(\omega(r^{2}+a^{2})-am\Xi)^{2}>0.

We obtain that

(5.31)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dr}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega\left((r^{2}+a^{2})\frac{d\Delta}{dr}-4r\Delta\right)-am\Xi\frac{d\Delta}{dr}}{\left((r^{2}+a^{2})\omega-am\Xi\right)^{3}}

\displaystyle=2\frac{\left(-\omega\Xi+\frac{2am\Xi}{l^{2}}\right)r^{3}+3M\omega r^{2}+\left(am\Xi\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}-a^{2}\Xi\omega-am\Xi\right)r-M\omega a^{2}+am\Xi M+am\Xi\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}}{\left((r^{2}+a^{2})\omega-am\Xi\right)^{3}}

\displaystyle\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu2\frac{N_{1}(r)}{\left((r^{2}+a^{2})\omega-am\Xi\right)^{3}}.

First, from (
5.31
) we note that

(5.32)

\frac{d^{2}N_{1}}{dr^{2}}=\omega\left(-6\frac{d\Delta}{dr}+\frac{\omega(r^{2}+a^{2})-am\Xi}{\omega}\frac{d^{3}\Delta}{dr^{3}}\right).

As mentioned earlier, in the non-superradiant frequencies (
5.24
) the inequality (
5.30
) holds, and therefore for all
r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
the function

(5.33)

\omega(r^{2}+a^{2})-am\Xi

has a sign. Therefore, by the first line of (
5.31
) and by the properties of the polynomial
\Delta
, see Lemma
2.1
, we conclude that

(5.34)

N_{1}(r_{+})\cdot N_{1}(\bar{r}_{+})<0.

Moreover, we calculate the following

(5.35)

\frac{dN_{1}}{dr}(r)=3(-\omega\Xi+2am\Xi\frac{1}{l^{2}})r^{2}+6M\omega r-a^{2}\Xi\omega-am\Xi+am\Xi\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}},

where the discriminant of (
5.35
) is

(5.36)

\text{discriminant of }\frac{dN_{1}}{dr}=(6M\omega)^{2}-12a\Xi^{2}\left(\omega-2\frac{am}{l^{2}}\right)\left(a\omega+m-m\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right).

Therefore, by using the discriminant (
5.36
) we note that the roots of
\frac{dN_{1}}{dr}
are the following two

(5.37)

\displaystyle\tilde{r}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{6M\omega\mp\sqrt{(6M\omega)^{2}-12a\Xi^{2}\left(\omega-2\frac{am}{l^{2}}\right)\left(a\omega+m-m\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}}{6\Xi\left(\omega-\frac{2ma}{l^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega}{\omega-\frac{2am}{l^{2}}}\frac{M}{\Xi}\mp\frac{\sqrt{(6M\omega)^{2}-12a\Xi^{2}\left(\omega-2\frac{am}{l^{2}}\right)\left(a\omega+m-m\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}}{6\Xi\left(\omega-\frac{2am}{l^{2}}\right)}.

We note from (
5.37
) that if
\frac{\omega}{\omega-\frac{2am}{l^{2}}}\leq 0
then at least one of
\tilde{r}_{\pm}
is negative, and therefore the function
\frac{dN_{1}}{dr}
attains at most one root in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
. It also follows that (
5.26
) holds since otherwise
\frac{dN_{1}}{dr}
would attain at least
2
roots in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
.

Therefore, without loss of generality we may assume that

(5.38)

\frac{\omega}{\omega-\frac{2am}{l^{2}}}>0.

We study the two disjoint frequency ranges

(5.39)

\omega\left(\omega(r^{2}+a^{2})-am\Xi\right)>0,\qquad\omega\left(\omega(r^{2}+a^{2})-am\Xi\right)<0

which cover the entire range of non-superradiant frequencies, except for
\omega=0
.

In the first case of (
5.39
) we proceed as follows. In view of the assumption (
5.38
) we note from (
5.32
) and from Lemma
2.1
(properties of
\Delta
) that the following holds

(5.40)

\omega\frac{d^{2}N_{1}}{dr^{2}}(r_{+})<0.

Therefore, inequality (
5.34
) in conjunction with (
5.40
) implies that the third degree polynomial function
N_{1}(r)
attains exactly one root in
(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
. Again, in view of that
N_{1}(r)
is a polynomial of degree
3
we also conclude (
5.26
). Therefore, we conclude the result of the Lemma under the first case of (
5.39
).

In the second case of (
5.39
) we proceed as follows. In view of the assumption (
5.38
) we note from (
5.32
) and from Lemma
2.1
that the following holds

(5.41)

\omega\frac{d^{2}N_{1}}{dr^{2}}(\bar{r}_{+})>0.

Therefore, inequality (
5.34
) in conjunction with (
5.41
) implies that the third degree polynomial function
N_{1}(r)
attains exactly one root in
(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
. Again, in view of that
N_{1}(r)
is a polynomial of degree
3
we also conclude (
5.26
). Therefore, we conclude the result of the Proposition under the second case of (
5.39
).
∎

5.3.
The definition of
g_{2}(\omega,m,r)

In view of Propositions
5.2
,
5.1
the following is well defined

Definition 5.2
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Let
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}
. Let
g_{1}(r),\penalty\ g_{2}^{2}(\omega,m,r)
be as in Definition
5.1
. Moreover, let
r_{\textit{crit}}(\omega,m)
be the unique critical point of the function
g_{2}^{2}(\omega,m,\cdot)
, as proved in Propositions
5.1
,
5.2
.

For the frequencies
am\omega>\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
we define

(5.42)

g_{2}(\omega,m,r)=\begin{cases}-\sqrt{g_{2}^{2}},\quad r\geq r_{\textit{crit}}(\omega,m)\\
+\sqrt{g_{2}^{2}},\quad r\leq r_{\textit{crit}}(\omega,m).\end{cases}

For the superradiant frequencies
(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF}
we define

(5.43)

g_{2}(\omega,m,r)=-\sqrt{g_{2}^{2}}=-\left|\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right|\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\sqrt{\Delta}}.

For the frequencies
am\omega<\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
we define

(5.44)

g_{2}(\omega,m,r)=\begin{cases}+\sqrt{g_{2}^{2}},\quad r\geq r_{\textit{crit}}(\omega,m)\\
-\sqrt{g_{2}^{2}},\quad r\leq r_{\textit{crit}}(\omega,m).\end{cases}

For the borderline non-superradiant frequencies
am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\penalty\ am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
we define respectively

(5.45)

g_{2}(\omega,m,r)=-\sqrt{g_{2}^{2}}=-|am\Xi|\frac{r^{2}-r_{+}^{2}}{(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2})\sqrt{\Delta}},\qquad g_{2}(\omega,m,r)=-\sqrt{g_{2}^{2}}=-|am\Xi|\frac{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}-r^{2}}{(\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2})\sqrt{\Delta}}.

In the Schwarzschild–de Sitter case
a=0
we define
g_{2}=g_{1}\frac{-i\omega}{\sqrt{1-9M^{2}\Lambda}}\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\sqrt{1+\frac{6M}{r}}
, as in our previous
[
31
]
. For the function
g_{2}(\omega,m,r)
graphically see Figure
1
.

5.4.
The definition of
\mathcal{G}

We define here the operator
\mathcal{G}
, discussed in the introduction, as follows

Definition 1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Let
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}
. Let the functions
g_{1},\penalty\ g_{2}
be as in Definitions
5.1
,
5.2
. For any
r_{+}<r<\bar{r}_{+}
we define the operator

(5.46)

\mathcal{G}=g_{1}(r)\partial_{r^{\star}}+i\textit{Op}(g_{2}),

such that the Fourier multiplier operator
\textit{Op}(g_{2})
is defined as

(5.47)

\left(\textit{Op}(g_{2})\Psi\right)(r;t,\varphi,\theta)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\omega t}e^{-im\varphi}g_{2}(\omega,m,r)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(\Psi)(r,\theta),

for any smooth
\Psi:(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})\times\mathbb{R}\times[0,2\pi)\times[0,\pi)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, where moreover
\Psi
is sufficiently integrable. For
\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}
see (
3.1
). For
r\leq r_{+}
and for
r\geq\bar{r}_{+}
we define
\mathcal{G}\equiv 0
, where
0
here is an operator.

For the Schwarzschild–de Sitter case
a=0
we note that the pseudodifferential operator induced from Definition
1
corresponds to the physical space vector field
\mathcal{G}
of our previous
[
31
]
.

If the solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
) is axisymmetric, then we will define the alternative physical space vector field

(5.48)

\mathcal{G}:=g_{1}(r)\partial_{r^{\star}}+G_{2}(r)\partial_{t^{\star}},

which we also denote as
\mathcal{G}\big|_{m=0}
, where

(5.49)

G_{2}=\begin{cases}\sqrt{G_{2}^{2}},\qquad r\geq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}\\
-\sqrt{G_{2}^{2}},\qquad r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}\end{cases}

with
G_{2}^{2}=\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}-\min_{[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}
, and
r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}
is the unique critical point of the function
\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}
.

5.5.
The properties of the function
g_{2}(\omega,m,r)

In the following Lemma we discuss the differentiability of the function
g_{2}(\omega,m,r)
, see Definition
5.2
. We will use the following Lemma in the ode estimates of Section
7
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Let the functions
g_{1},g_{2}
be as in Definitions
5.1
,
5.2
respectively. Then, the following hold.

(1)

For fixed
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}
we have
g_{2}(\omega,m,\cdot)\in C^{0}(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})

(2)

For fixed
(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c}
we have that
g_{2}(\omega,m,\cdot)\in C^{1}(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
.

(3)

For fixed
(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF}
we have that
g_{2}(\omega,m,\cdot)\not\in C^{1}(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
. Specifically, the function
g_{2}^{\prime}(\omega,m,\cdot)
is piecewise
C^{0}(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
and is not differentiable only at the point
r_{s}
where
am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{s}^{2}+a^{2}}
, see Figure
1
.

(4)

For fixed
m\in\mathbb{Z}
and
r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
and for any
\omega
such that
(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c}
we have
g_{2}(\cdot,m,r)
is differentiable.

(5)

For fixed
r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
and fixed
m\in\mathbb{Z}
the function
g_{2}(\cdot,m,r)
is not differentiable in
\omega
at the point
\omega=\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}
.

(6)

For a smooth function
u
satisfying the outgoing boundary conditions (
3.17
) we obtain

(5.50)

\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}(\omega,m,r)\right)\Big|_{r^{\star}=\pm\infty}u=0.

(7)

For the non-superradiant frequencies
(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c}
we have

(5.51)

\frac{1}{g_{1}}\left|\frac{dg_{2}}{dr^{\star}}\right|\geq b\left(\left|\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right|+\Delta\left(|\omega|+\frac{|am\Xi|}{r^{2}}\right)\right),

Proof.

We obtain the following:

(1)

Follows from the definition of
g_{2}
, see Definition
5.2
.

(2)

Follows from the definition of
g_{2}
, see Definition
5.2
for the non superradiant frequencies
(\mathcal{SF})^{c}
. Specifically, since
g_{2}^{2}(\omega,m,\cdot)\in C^{\infty}(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
, see Proposition
5.2
, we use Taylor’s theorem around the point
r=r_{crit}
, for the cases
r_{crit}\neq r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}
which correspond to the borderline superradiant cases
am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
respectively. We obtain

(5.52)

g_{2}(\omega,m,r)=\pm(r-r_{crit})\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\big|\frac{d^{2}}{dr^{2}}\big|_{r=r_{crit}}\left(g_{2}^{2}(\omega,m,r)\right)\big|+\mathcal{O}(r-r_{crit})}.

Since
\big|\frac{d^{2}}{dr^{2}}\big|_{r=r_{crit}}(g_{2}^{2})\big|>0
, see Proposition
5.2
, we conclude that indeed
g_{2}
is indeed
C^{1}
in a neighborgood of
r_{crit}
. Away from
r_{crit}
the function
g_{2}
is obviously
C^{1}
from its definition.

(3)

Follows from the definition of
g_{2}
, see Definition
5.2
. Specifically, we see that the function
g_{2}(\omega,m,r)
is not differentiable at the point
r=r_{crit}=r_{s}
, where
am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{s}^{2}+a^{2}}
, see Proposition
5.1
.

(4)

Follows from the definition of
g_{2}
, see Definition
5.2
.

(5)

Follows from the definition of
g_{2}
, see Definition
5.2
.

(6)

Follows from the definitions of
g_{1},g_{2}
, see Definition
5.2
, by also using the outgoing boundary condition (
3.17
).

(7)

We first note that for the borderline non-superradiant frequencies

am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\qquad am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}

we have respectively

g_{2}(\omega,m,r)=-|am\Xi|\frac{r^{2}-r_{+}^{2}}{(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2})\sqrt{\Delta}},\qquad g_{2}(\omega,m,r)=-|am\Xi|\frac{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}-r^{2}}{(\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2})\sqrt{\Delta}}.

see Definition
5.2
, for which (
5.51
) indeed holds after a direct computation. Which indeed satisfies the lower bound (
5.51
). To prove that the remaining frequencies satisfy the bound (
5.51
) is a trivial computation. The reader should also see Figure
1
for more intuition.

∎

5.6.
Parseval identities with the operator
\mathcal{G}

Let
g_{1}(r),g_{2}(\omega,m,r),\mathcal{G},\Psi
be as in Definition
1
. Let
v
be as follows

v=(g_{1}\partial_{r^{\star}}+ig_{2}(\omega,m,r))u,

where
u=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(\Psi)
. For
\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}
see (
3.1
).

Then, we have the following Parseval identities

(5.53)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}|v|^{2}d\omega

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\mathcal{G}\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi\right)|^{2}dtd\sigma,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\omega^{2}|v|^{2}d\omega

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{t}\mathcal{G}\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi\right)|^{2}dtd\sigma,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}m^{2}|v|^{2}d\omega

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{\varphi}\mathcal{G}\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi\right)|^{2}dtd\sigma,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\tilde{\lambda}|v|^{2}d\omega

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|^{d\sigma}\nabla\mathcal{G}\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi\right)|^{2}dtd\sigma,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}(r-r_{+})^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)v\right|^{2}d\omega

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r-r_{+})^{-1}\left|(\partial_{r^{\star}}-(\partial_{t}+\omega_{+}\partial_{\varphi}))\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi)\right|^{2}dtd\sigma,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}(r-\bar{r}_{+})^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)v\right|^{2}d\omega

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r-\bar{r}_{+})^{-1}\left|(\partial_{r^{\star}}+(\partial_{t}+\bar{\omega}_{+}\partial_{\varphi}))\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi)\right|^{2}dtd\sigma,

for any
r_{+}<r<\bar{r}_{+}
, where
d\sigma
is the standard metric of the unit sphere, and
{}^{d\sigma}\nabla
is the covariant derivative of
d\sigma
. Recall that
\omega_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\bar{\omega}_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
.

5.7.
The operator
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
and its properties

We define a more regular version of
\mathcal{G}
, which we call
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
, see already Definition
5.4
. The reason for introducing
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
is to be able to appeal to pseudodifferential estimates which require operators with sufficiently regular symbol, see Lemma
A.1
.

Specifically, recall from the definition of
g_{2}(\omega,m,r)
, see Definition
5.2
, that for superradiant frequencies we have

(5.54)

g_{2}(\omega,m,r)=-\left|\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right|\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\sqrt{\Delta}},

and therefore, for fixed
m
and
r
the function
g_{2}(\cdot,m,r)
is not differentiable in
\omega
.

5.7.1.
The function
\widetilde{g_{2}}

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. We define a function

(5.55)

\tilde{\epsilon}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},

such that
\tilde{\epsilon}(\cdot,m)
is smooth for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, and the following hold:

(5.56)

\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)\equiv 0,\quad(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c},

(5.57)

0<|\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)|\leq C,\quad(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF},

(5.58)

|\partial_{\omega}^{i_{1}}\Delta_{m}^{i_{2}}\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)|\leq C(1+|\omega|)^{-i_{1}-i_{2}}(1+|m|)^{-i_{1}-i_{2}},\qquad(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF},

for all
0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 1
, where
\Delta_{m}
is the finite difference operator. The constant
C=C(a,M,l)
is independent of
\omega,m,i_{1},i_{2}
.

Note that in the Schwarzschild–de Sitter case
a=0
we have that
\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)\equiv 0
.

Remark 5.2
.

We here give an example of a function that satisfies (
5.56
), (
5.57
), (
5.58
). Let
0\leq\lambda_{1}\leq\lambda_{2}
. We define the smooth bump function

(5.59)

f_{a,\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(x)=\begin{cases}(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})e^{\frac{1}{(x-\lambda_{1})(x-\lambda_{2})}},\quad\lambda_{1}\leq x\leq\lambda_{2}\\
0,\quad\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

In the interval
[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]
we have the derivative

(5.60)

f^{\prime}_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(x)=-\frac{\left(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}\right)e^{\frac{1}{\left(x-\lambda_{1}\right)\left(x-\lambda_{2}\right)}}\left(\left(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}\right)-2x\right)}{\left(x-\lambda_{1}\right){}^{2}\left(x-\lambda_{2}\right){}^{2}}.

Now, we give an example of a function that satisfies (
5.56
), (
5.57
), (
5.58
). Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. We define the function

(5.61)

\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)=\begin{cases}f_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}\left(\left|\frac{\omega}{m}\right|\right),\qquad(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF}\\
0,\qquad(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c}\end{cases}

where
\lambda_{1}=\frac{|a|\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\penalty\ \lambda_{2}=\frac{|a|\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
. For the case
m=0
it is clear we can simply define
\tilde{\epsilon}\equiv 0
.

Now, we define the following

Definition 5.3
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Let
\tilde{\epsilon}(\cdot,\cdot)
be as in (
5.55
). We define the following function

\displaystyle\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,r)

\displaystyle\equiv g_{2}(\omega,m,r),\qquad(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c},

\displaystyle\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,r)

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}\left(\omega-\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}m\right)^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)},\qquad(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF}

where for
g_{1}(r),\penalty\ g_{2}(\omega,m,r)
see Definition
5.2
. In the Schwarzschild–de Sitter case
a=0
we define
\widetilde{g}_{2}\equiv g_{2}
.

5.7.2.
The definition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}

In view of Definition
5.3
we define the more regular version of
\mathcal{G}
, see Definition
1
, as follows

Definition 5.4
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Let
g_{1},\widetilde{g_{2}}
be as in Definition
5.3
. For any
r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
we define the following operator

(5.62)

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}=g_{1}\partial_{r^{\star}}+i\textit{Op}(\widetilde{g_{2}}),

such that the Fourier multiplier operator
Op(\widetilde{g_{2}})
is defined as

(5.63)

\left(\textit{Op}(\widetilde{g_{2}})\Psi\right)(r;t,\varphi,\theta)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\omega t}e^{-im\varphi}\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,r)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(\Psi)(r,\theta),

for any smooth
\Psi:(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})\times\mathbb{R}\times[0,2\pi)\times[0,\pi)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, where moreover
\Psi
is sufficiently integrable. For
\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}
see (
3.1
). For
r\leq r_{+}
and for
r\geq\bar{r}_{+}
we define
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\equiv 0
, where
0
here is an operator.

5.7.3.
Properties of the function
\widetilde{g_{2}}

We prove here several properties of
\widetilde{g_{2}}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Let
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}
. Let
g_{1},\widetilde{g_{2}}
be as in Definition
5.3
. Then, the following hold

(1)

For any
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}
we have
\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,\cdot)\in C^{1}(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
.

(2)

For any
r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
and for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have that
\widetilde{g_{2}}(\cdot,m,r)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R})
.

(3)

For a smooth function
u
that satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions (
3.17
) we have

\left(g_{1}(r)\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+i\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,r)\right)\Big|_{r^{\star}=\pm\infty}u=0.

(4)

For any
r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
the symbols

(5.64)

-g_{1}(r)i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)+i\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,r),\qquad g_{1}(r)i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)+i\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,r)

belong in the classical symbol class

S^{1}=\{\sigma:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\exists C>0\penalty\ \forall 0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 1\penalty\ \left|\partial^{i_{1}}_{\omega}\Delta^{i_{2}}_{m}\sigma(\omega,m)\right|\leq C\left(1+|\omega|\right)^{1-i_{1}-i_{2}}\left(1+|m|\right)^{1-i_{1}-i_{2}}\},

where the constant
C>0
is independent of
r
and
blows up
in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
. Note that for
a=0
the symbols (
5.64
) also belong in the symbol class
S^{1}
, see the definition of
\widetilde{g}_{2}
. See already Appendix
A
for the Coifman–Meyer commutation for operators in the above symbol class.

(5)

For any
r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
the symbol
\widetilde{g_{2}}
belongs in the symbol class
S^{1}
, again for constants
C(a)
independent of
r
, and
blow up
in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
. For
a=0
the symbol
\widetilde{g}_{2}
belongs in the symbol class
S^{1}

(6)

There exists a constant
C(a,M,l)>0
, such that
0<C(0,M,l)<\infty
, such that for any
r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
and for any
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}
we have

(5.65)

|\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,r)-g_{2}(\omega,m,r)|\leq C.

Proof.

First, we discuss the fifth statement. It is easy to see from the definition of
\widetilde{g}_{2}
, see Definition
5.3
, that indeed it belongs in the symbol class
S^{1}
. Furthermore, the constant in the Coifman Meyer commutations blows up in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
since we have

(5.66)

\partial_{\omega}\widetilde{g}_{2}=-\frac{2g_{1}^{2}\left(\omega-\frac{a\Xi m}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)+\partial_{\omega}\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}{2\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}\left(\omega-\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}m\right)^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}},

where we need to recall the definition of
\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)
from Section
5.7.1
. Note, however, that for
a=0
we have
\widetilde{g}_{2}\equiv g_{2}
and therefore we have that in the case
a=0
the symbol
\widetilde{g}_{2}
belongs in the symbol class
S^{1}
for
r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
.

Now, we will address the result that the symbols (
5.64
) belong in the symbol class
S^{1}
, namely the second statement of the present Lemma. Then, we will prove the estimate (
5.65
). The remaining statements follow easily from the definition of
\widetilde{g_{2}}
, see Definition
5.3
.

To prove that the operators (
5.64
) belong in the symbol class
S^{1}
we proceed as follows. We only discuss the first symbol of (
5.64
) as the proof for the second is the same.

We write

(5.67)

\displaystyle-g_{1}(r)i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)+i\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,r)=g_{1}(r)iR_{1}(\omega,m,r).

We recall from Propositions
5.1
,
5.2
that for any frequency
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}
we have a unique critical point
r_{crit}(\omega,m)
of the function

(5.68)

\mathcal{T}(\omega,m,r)=\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}.

In view of Definition
5.3
, for the non-superradiant frequencies
am\omega>\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
and
am\omega<\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
we have respectively

(5.69)

R_{1}(\omega,m,r)=-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)+\begin{cases}-\sqrt{\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}-\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\min_{r}\mathcal{T}(\omega,m,r)},\penalty\ \penalty\ r\geq r_{crit}\\
+\sqrt{\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}-\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\min_{r}\mathcal{T}(\omega,m,r)},\penalty\ \penalty\ r\leq r_{crit}\end{cases}

and

(5.70)

\displaystyle R_{1}(\omega,m,r)

\displaystyle=-\left(\omega-\frac{am^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)+\begin{cases}+\sqrt{\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}-\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\min_{r}\mathcal{T}(\omega,m,r)},\quad r\geq r_{\textit{crit}}\\
-\sqrt{\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}-\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\min_{r}\mathcal{T}(\omega,m,r)},\quad r\leq r_{\textit{crit}}.\end{cases}

and for the enlarged superradiant frequencies
am\omega\in[\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}]
we have

(5.71)

\displaystyle R_{1}(\omega,m,r)

\displaystyle=-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)-\sqrt{\left(\omega-\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}m\right)^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\textAlpha(\omega,m)},

\displaystyle A(\omega,m)

\displaystyle=-\min_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\left(g_{1}^{2}\left(\omega-\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}m\right)^{2}\right)+\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m).

For the non-superradiant frequencies
am\omega>\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
and
am\omega<\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
the result that (
5.64
) belong in the symbol class
S^{1}
is immediate by simply differentiating (
5.69
) and (
5.70
).

For the enlarged superradiant frequencies
am\omega\in[\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}]
we have

(5.72)

\partial_{\omega}R_{1}(\omega,m,r)=-\frac{\frac{\Delta(r)\partial_{\omega}A(\omega,m)}{\left(a^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{2}}+2\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{a^{2}+r^{2}}\right)}{2\sqrt{\frac{\Delta(r)A(\omega,m)}{\left(a^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{2}}+\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{a^{2}+r^{2}}\right)^{2}}}-1.

We note that there exists a constant
C(a,M,l)
such that for any
r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
and for any
am\omega\in[\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}]
we have

(5.73)

|\partial_{\omega}R_{1}(\omega,m)|\leq C(a),\qquad|R_{1}(\omega,m+1)-R_{1}(\omega,m)|\leq C(a)

in view also of the definition of
\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)
, see Definition
5.3
. We conclude that for the enlarged superradiant frequencies
am\omega\in[\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}]
we indeed have that
R_{1}\in S^{1}
. It is easy to prove that for any
r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
we have that
g_{1}(r)iR_{1}(\cdot,\cdot)\in S^{1}
. Note that the constant
C
of (
5.73
) blows up in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
, in view of the definition of
\epsilon(\omega,m)
, see Section
5.7.1
.

Now, we discuss (
5.65
). For
(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c}
the result is trivial since
\widetilde{g_{2}}\equiv g_{2}
. Therefore, we study the case
(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF}
. For brevity we will use the notation
\Omega(r)=\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}
. We have the following identity

(5.74)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{g_{2}}-g_{2}|

\displaystyle=\left|\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}-|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\right|

\displaystyle=1_{|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\leq 10}\left|\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}-|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\right|

\displaystyle+1_{|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\geq 10}\left|\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}-|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\right|

\displaystyle=1_{|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\leq 10}\left|\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}-|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\right|

\displaystyle+1_{|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\geq 10}\left|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)\right|\left(\sqrt{1+\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}}}-1\right).

Now, since
\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)}<1
in the last term of (
5.74
) we use Taylor’s theorem and we write (
5.74
) as follows

(5.75)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{g_{2}}-g_{2}|

\displaystyle=1_{|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\leq 10}\left|\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}-|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\right|

\displaystyle+1_{|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\geq 10}\left|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)\right|\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=1_{|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\leq 10}\left|\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}-|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\right|

\displaystyle+1_{|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\geq 10}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}{g_{1}|\omega-\Omega(r)m|}+\left|g_{1}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)\right|\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}}\right)^{2}\right),

where there exists a constant
C(a,M,l)>0
such that for any
r,\omega,m
such that
|g_{1}(r)(\omega-\Omega(r)m)|\geq 10
we have that

(5.76)

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)}{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}}\right)^{2}\leq C\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}^{2}(\omega,m)}{g_{1}^{2}(\omega-\Omega(r)m)^{2}}.

Now, the result (
5.65
) is immediate from (
5.74
) and (
5.75
) in view of the definition of the function
\tilde{\epsilon}(\omega,m)
, see (
5.55
). We conclude the lemma.
∎

6.
The main Theorems

In this Section we present our main theorems.

Theorem 1

(detailed version)
.

Let
l>0
,
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
and

(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}},

where for
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
see (
4.3
).

Then, there exists a constant

(6.1)

C(a,M,l,\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}})>0

such that for any
T\geq 3
and for any
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
such that
\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{1}+T<\tau_{2}
, where for
\tilde{T}
see (
1.38
), then the following hold. (Recall from (
1.38
) that
\tilde{T}=T^{2}-2T-1
).

Let
\psi
satisfy the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
) in
D(\tau_{1}-\tilde{T},\tau_{2})=\supp\chi_{+}\cup\supp\chi_{-}
, where for the cut-offs

(6.2)

\chi_{+}(t^{\star})=\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}(t^{\star}),\qquad\chi_{-}(t^{\star})=\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}(t^{\star}+\tilde{T})

see Section
2.11
, and for their graphic representation see Figure
9
.

Then, the following estimate holds

(6.3)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi\right|^{2}+\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi,\psi)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi,\psi),

for any
\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}
, where for the operator
\mathcal{G}
see Section
5
. We used the notation
\mathcal{G}_{\chi}=\chi\mathcal{G}\chi
. Note that
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
. For the energy density
\mathcal{E}(\cdot,\cdot)
see (
1.40
).

Proof.

See Section
8
.
∎

Remark 6.1
.

For fixed
(a,M,l)
the constant in the RHS of (
6.3
) blow up in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
. Note, however, that
a=0
is indeed allowed in the theorem, i.e. the constant for
a=0
is finite. This bad behaviour of our constant stems from the non-differentiability of the
g_{2}
, specifically see Lemma
5.1
where we study the smoothed version
\widetilde{g}_{2}
.

For a graphic representation of the cut-offs
\chi_{+},\chi_{-}
of Theorem
1
see Figure
9
.

Figure 9.
The cut-offs
\chi_{+},\chi_{-}
(not to scale)

Exponential decay follows as a Corollary of Theorem
1
.

Corollary 1.1

(detailed version)
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
1
be satisfied, where now
\psi
satisfies the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
) in the domain
D(0,\infty)
instead. Then, there exist constants

(6.4)

C=C(a,M,l,\mu_{KG}),\qquad c=c(a,M,l,\mu_{KG})

independent of
\psi
, such that for any
T\geq 3
sufficiently large, then the following hold.

Let

(6.5)

\chi_{\textit{data}}(t^{\star})=\chi_{0,T^{2}}(t^{\star}).

Then, for any
\tau\geq 0
we have the following

(6.6)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq C\Bigg(\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{\supp\chi_{\textit{data}}}d\tau^{\prime}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{\textit{data}}}\psi,\psi)\Bigg)\cdot e^{-c\tau}.

Furthermore, we have the following pointwise decay

(6.7)

\displaystyle\sup_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}|\psi-\underline{\psi}|\leq C\sqrt{E}e^{-c\tau}

where
E=\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}[n^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}[n^{3}\psi]n^{\mu}
and the constant
\underline{\psi}
is either
\underline{\psi}=0
if
\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}>0
, or
|\underline{\psi}|\leq|\underline{\psi}(0)|+C\sqrt{E}
, if
\mu_{\textit{KG}}=0
.

Proof.

See Section
9
.
∎

Note the following Remarks

Remark 6.2
.

If the solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
) is in addition axisymmetric, then we obtain the result of Theorem
1
for all subextremal parameters
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and all Klein–Gordon masses
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. See Section
11
.

Remark 6.3
.

Note that the integral quantities on the right hand side of (
6.6
) can be controlled by a Sobolev energy on the initial data hypersurface
\{t^{\star}=0\}
, as follows

(6.8)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{\supp\chi_{\textit{data}}}d\tau^{\prime}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{\textit{data}}}\psi,\psi)

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+B\int\int_{\supp\chi_{data}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{\textit{data}}}\psi,\psi)\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n^{2}\psi]n^{\mu},

where in (
6.8
) we only appeal to Parseval and pointwise bounds, without using that
\psi
is a solution of the Klein–Gordon equation. To prove (
6.8
) we appealed to the coarea formula, see Section
2.9
, and standard finite in time energy estimates for the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
), see our companion
[
28
]
.

We also have the following ‘relatively non degenerate’ estimate for the inhomogeneous Klein–Gordon equation in a
\delta
-extended region bounded between the spacelike hypersurfaces
\mathcal{H}^{+}_{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}_{\delta}
and the hypersurfaces
\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\},\penalty\ \{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}
, see Definition
2.5
.

Theorem 2
.

Let
l>0
,
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
and

(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}},

where for
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
see (
4.3
).

Then, there exist constants

(6.9)

C(a,M,l,\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}})>0,\qquad\delta(a,M,l,\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}})>0,

such that for any
T\geq 3
and for any
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
such that
\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{1}+T<\tau_{2}
then the following hold.

Let
\psi
satisfy the inhomogeneous Klein–Gordon equation

(6.10)

\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu^{2}_{KG}\psi=F

on
D_{\delta}(\tau_{1}-\tilde{T},\tau_{2})
, where
F
is a sufficiently regular function.

Let
\{t^{\star}=\tau\}
be as in Definition
2.5
. Then, the following holds

(6.11)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+\int\int_{D_{\delta}(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\left|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C(\delta)}{T}\int\int_{D_{\delta}(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+C(\delta)\int\int_{\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}\Delta|\mathcal{G}\left(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}F\right)|^{2}+|\eta\chi_{+}^{2}F|^{2},

for any
\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}
, where for
\mathcal{G}
see Definition
1
, and we used the notation
\mathcal{G}_{\chi}=\chi\mathcal{G}\chi
. Moreover, for the cut-offs
\eta=\eta^{(T)}_{\tau_{1}},\penalty\ \chi_{+}=\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}},\penalty\ \chi_{-}(t^{\star})=\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}(t^{\star}+\tilde{T})
, see Section
2.11
and for the graphic representation of the latter two cut-offs see Figure
9
.

Proof.

See Section
10
.
∎

We have the following higher order Corollary

Corollary 2.1
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
2
hold. Then, for any
j\geq 3
there exists a constant
C=C(j,a,M,l)>0
and there exists a sufficiently small
\delta(a,M,l,\mu_{KG},j)>0
, such that for any
T\geq 3
, we have

(6.12)

\displaystyle E_{j-1}[\psi](\tau)+\int\int_{D_{\delta}(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}\frac{1}{\Delta}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq j-2}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}W^{1+i_{2}}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi\right|^{2}+\int_{\tau_{1}+T}^{\tau_{2}}d\tau^{\prime}E_{\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}},j}[\psi]d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq CE_{j-1}[\psi](\tau_{1})+\frac{C}{T}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{1}+T}E_{\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}},j}[\psi](\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle+C\int\int_{\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}\Delta\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq j-2}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}W^{i_{2}}\mathcal{G}\left(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}F\right)\right|^{2}+\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j-2}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}W^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\left(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}F\right)\right|^{2},

for any
\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}
, with

(6.13)

\displaystyle E_{j}[\psi](\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t^{\star}}^{i_{2}}\left(Z^{\star}\right)^{i_{3}}\psi\right|^{2}dg_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}},

\displaystyle E_{\mathcal{G}_{\chi},j}[\psi](\tau)

\displaystyle=\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq j-1}\Delta\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}W^{i_{2}}Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}_{\chi}\psi\right|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq j-1}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}W^{i_{2}}\mathcal{G}_{\chi}\psi\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j-1}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}W^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi\right|^{2},

where
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
. Moreover, for the spacetime domain
\mathcal{M}_{\delta}
see Section
2.8
.

Proof.

See Section
10
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

Note that at top oder in (
6.12
), we only include
W,\not{\nabla}
derivatives of
\mathcal{G}_{\chi}\psi
, because of the bad behavior of high
Z^{\star}
derivatives of
\mathcal{G}_{\chi}\psi
. Specifically, note from Proposition
5.3
that
(Z^{\star})^{2}\mathcal{G}_{\chi}\psi
can only be defined in a distributional sense and therefore
\int\int|(Z^{\star})^{2}\mathcal{G}_{\chi}\psi|^{2}
is not well defined.

7.
The fixed frequency ode estimate: Theorem
7.1

The main result of the present Section is Theorem
7.1
. As mentioned already in the introduction, at low order, we need the Morawetz estimate of Theorem
4.1
with the sharp degeneration at
r_{trap}(\omega,m,\ell)
, where
\ell
is the Carter frequency. Note, however, that the functions
g_{2}(\omega,m,r)
,
f_{s}(\omega,m,r)
,
h_{s}(\omega,m,r)
that will be used in the present Section, see Definition
5.2
and (
7.37
), (
7.44
) respectively, do not depend on the Carter frequency
\ell
.

For convenience we will use the notation

(^{\prime}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}).

In the main estimates of the present Section we track the weights in
r
at the top order of our estimates, for the readers interested in the asymptotically flat case.

7.1.
The constants

We use the constants

(7.1)

b(a,M,l,\mu_{\textit{KG}})>0,\qquad B(a,M,l,\mu_{\textit{KG}})>0

with the algebra of constants

(7.2)

b+b=b,\qquad b\cdot b=b,\qquad B+B=B,\qquad B\cdot B=B

7.2.
Carter’s radial ode with outgoing boundary conditions

In the present Section we study smooth solutions of the inhomogeneous radial ode

(7.3)

u^{\prime\prime}+\left(\omega^{2}-V\right)u=H

that satisfy outgoing boundary conditions

(7.4)

\displaystyle u^{\prime}=-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)u,\qquad u^{\prime}=i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)u,

at
r^{\star}=-\infty
,
r^{\star}=\infty
respectively. We also assume that
H(r)
is a smooth function in
(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
.

7.3.
The commuted quantity
v

Note the following definition

Definition 7.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Let
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}
. Let
u
be a smooth function. Moreover, let
g_{1}(r^{\star}),g_{2}(\omega,m,r^{\star})
be as in Definition
5.2
. Then, we define the following

(7.5)

v=\left(g_{1}(r^{\star})\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}(\omega,m,r^{\star})\right)u.

In view of the definitions of
g_{1},g_{2}
, see Definition
5.2
, then we obtain that for a smooth
u
satisfying outgoing boundary condition (
7.4
), we also conclude that

(7.6)

v|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}=v|_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}=0,

see Proposition
5.3
.

7.4.
The main fixed frequency ode result, Theorem
7.1

We prove the following result

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
.
Then, there exists a sufficiently small

(7.7)

\epsilon(a,M,l,\mu_{KG})>0

such that for any
\omega\in\mathbb{R},m\in\mathbb{Z},\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}
the following holds.

Let
u
be a smooth solution of the inhomogeneous radial ode (
7.3
), with smooth inhomogeneity
H
, that moreover satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions (
7.4
). Let
v
be as in Definition
7.1
. Then, the following energy estimate holds

(7.8)

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}(r-r_{+})^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}(\bar{r}_{+}-r)^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}}{r^{3}}|v|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+\Delta^{2}\omega^{2}|u|^{2}\right)dr

\displaystyle\leq B\Bigg(\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}-(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(1_{\{|m|>0\}}|u|^{2}+\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}(\omega,m,\ell)}{r}\right)^{2}(\omega+\tilde{\lambda})|u|^{2}\right)dr\Bigg)

\displaystyle+B\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+B\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

where
\tilde{\lambda}=\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+(a\omega)^{2}
, see Definition
3.2
, where
\omega_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\penalty\ \bar{\omega}_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
. For the trapping parameter
r_{trap}=r_{trap}(\omega,m,\ell)
see the Morawetz estimate of Theorem
4.1
.

Proof.

See Section
7.9
.
∎

To prove Theorem
7.1
we need several preparatory definitions and results, which are detailed in the following Sections.

7.5.
Preparatory identities

We have the following Lemmata

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\omega\in\mathbb{R},m\in\mathbb{Z},\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}
. Let
u
be a smooth solution of the inhomogeneous radial ode (
7.3
) that satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions (
7.4
).

Then, the radial function
v
, see Definition
7.1
, is a
C^{0}(-\infty,+\infty)
function and the derivative
v^{\prime}
is a piesewise
C^{1}(-\infty,+\infty)
function. Specifically, if
(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF}
then
v
is not differentiable at
r=r_{s}(\omega,m)
, where
am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{s}^{2}+a^{2}}
.

Moreover, the following equation holds

(7.9)

\displaystyle v^{\prime\prime}+\left(\omega^{2}-V\right)v=2i\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}v+\left(g_{1}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}u+\frac{v}{g_{1}}(V_{\textit{SL}}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}})^{\prime}\right)+\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right)

in a distributional sense.

Proof.

We recall from Proposition
5.3
that for the superradiant frequencies
\mathcal{SF}
that the function
g_{2}(\omega,m,r)
is
C^{0}(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
and the derivative
g_{2}^{\prime}
is piecewise
C^{0}
. Specifically, the function
g_{2}^{\prime}
is not differentiable only at the point
r=r_{s}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
, where
am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{s}^{2}+a^{2}}
, see Proposition
5.3
. It is easy to see from the Definition
7.1
that, for the superradiant frequencies
\mathcal{SF}
, the function
v
is also a
C^{0}(-\infty,+\infty)
function, where its derivative is piecewise
C^{0}
.

For the non superradiant frequencies
(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c}
we have that
g_{2}(\omega,m,\cdot)\in C^{1}(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
, see Proposition
5.3
. It is easy to see from Definition
7.1
that, for the non-superradiant frequencies
(\mathcal{SF})^{c}
, we have
v\in C^{1}(-\infty,+\infty)
.

Now, by recalling the definitions of
g_{1},g_{2}
, see Definition
5.2
, we compute

(7.10)

\displaystyle v^{\prime\prime}-\left(\omega^{2}-V\right)v=

\displaystyle\left(g_{1}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}u+\frac{v}{g_{1}}(V_{\textit{SL}}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}})^{\prime}\right)+\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right)-\frac{v}{g_{1}}\left(g_{1}^{2}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}-g_{2}^{2}\right)^{\prime}

\displaystyle+2i\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}v,

where the above is to be understood in a distributional sense, since
v
is not differentiable in
r
for certain frequencies
(\omega,m,\ell)
.
∎

We need the following energy identity for
v
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let

(7.11)

(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF}.

Let
u
be a smooth solution of the inhomogeneous radial ode (
7.3
) that satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions (
7.4
). Let
v
be as in Definition
7.1
.

Then, we have

(7.12)

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}f_{s}(r)|v|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}\left(g_{1}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}u\right)\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}v^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right)\right)dr^{\star},

for any
f_{s}\in C^{\infty}[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
with
f_{s}(r_{s})=0
, where
r_{s}(\omega,m)
is such that
am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{s}^{2}+a^{2}}
.

Proof.

We multiply the equation satisfied by
v
, see Lemma
7.1
, with

(7.13)

f_{s}(r)\bar{v},\qquad f_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{r}[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}].

We obtain

(7.14)

\displaystyle f_{s}(r)\bar{v}\left(v^{\prime\prime}-\left(\omega^{2}-V\right)v\right)

\displaystyle=2i\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}f_{s}(r)|v|^{2}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\bar{v}u+f_{s}(r)g_{1}^{\prime\prime}\bar{v}u^{\prime}+f_{s}(r)\frac{|v|^{2}}{g_{1}}(V_{\textit{SL}}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle+f_{s}(r)\bar{v}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right).

Then, we take imaginary parts to (
7.14
) and obtain

(7.15)

\displaystyle\imaginary\left(f_{s}(r)\bar{v}v^{\prime\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=2\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}f_{s}(r)|v|^{2}+g_{2}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(iu\bar{v}\right)+g_{1}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}u^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right)\right).

Now, we integrate (
7.15
) with measure
dr^{\star}
and after the appropriate integration by parts we obtain

(7.16)

\displaystyle\imaginary\left[f_{s}(r)\bar{v}v^{\prime}\right]^{\infty}_{-\infty}+\imaginary\left[f_{s}(r)\bar{v}v^{\prime}\right]^{r_{s}+}_{r_{s}-}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}v^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}f_{s}(r)|v|^{2}dr^{\star}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}g_{2}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(iu\bar{v}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}g_{1}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}u^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right)\right)dr^{\star}.

In view of that
u
satisfies outgoing boundary conditions (
7.4
) then we have

(7.17)

\left[f_{s}(r(r^{\star}))\bar{v}v^{\prime}\right]^{\infty}_{-\infty}=0

see Proposition
5.3
. Moreover, by the assumption that
f_{s}(r_{s})=0
we have that
\left[f_{s}(r)\bar{v}v^{\prime}\right]^{r_{s}+}_{r_{s}-}=0
. Therefore, we obtain that equation (
7.16
) implies

(7.18)

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}f_{s}(r)|v|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}g_{2}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(iu\bar{v}\right)dr^{\star}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}v^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}g_{1}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}u^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right)\right)dr^{\star}.

We conclude (
7.12
) and the proof.

∎

7.6.
Preparatory energy estimate for
v
for the non superradiant frequencies
(\mathcal{SF})^{c}

We need the following estimate for the non-superradiant frequencies

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let

(7.19)

(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c}.

Let
u
be a smooth solution of the inhomogeneous radial ode (
7.3
) that satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions (
7.4
). Let
v
be as in Definition
7.1
.

We have

(7.20)

\displaystyle b(a,M,l)\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{3}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)\right)|v|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|\omega|+|am\Xi|\right)|v|\left|g_{1}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}u\right|dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}r\left|2g_{1}^{\prime}H+(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right|^{2}dr^{\star}.

Proof.

We multiply the equation that
v
satisfies, see Lemma
7.1
, namely

(7.21)

\displaystyle v^{\prime\prime}+\left(\omega^{2}-V\right)v=2i\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}v+\left(g_{1}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}u+\frac{v}{g_{1}}(V_{\textit{SL}}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}})^{\prime}\right)+\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right),

with

(7.22)

-\text{sign}\left(\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}\right)\bar{v}

in view of that
|\frac{dg_{2}}{dr^{\star}}|\geq\Delta(|\omega+|am\Xi|)
for the non-superradiant frequencies, see Proposition
5.3
, and then take imaginary parts. Finally, we integrate and obtain

(7.23)

\displaystyle-\text{sign}\left(\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}\right)\imaginary[\bar{v}v^{\prime}]^{\infty}_{-\infty}+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}\right||v|^{2}dr^{\star}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|v|\left|g_{1}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}u\right|+|v|\left|2g_{1}^{\prime}H+(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right|\right)dr^{\star}.

Since
u
satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions (
7.4
) we can immediately compute that
v
also satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions

(7.24)

v^{\prime}=\begin{cases}-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)v,\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur^{\star}=-\infty\\
i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)v,\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur^{\star}=\infty.\end{cases}

Therefore, we compute

(7.25)

\displaystyle\imaginary[\bar{v}v^{\prime}]^{\infty}_{-\infty}=\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)|v|^{2}(\infty)+\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)|v|^{2}(-\infty)

where note that
|v|(\pm\infty)=0
, by Proposition
5.3
. We obtain

(7.26)

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}\right||v|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|v|\left|g_{1}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}u\right|+|v|\left|2g_{1}^{\prime}H+(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right|\right)dr^{\star}.

We note from Proposition
5.3
that the following holds

(7.27)

\frac{1}{g_{1}}\left|\frac{dg_{2}}{dr^{\star}}\right|\geq b\left(\left|\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right|+\Delta\left(|\omega|+\frac{|am\Xi|}{r^{2}}\right)\right).

We multiply both sides of (
7.26
) with

(7.28)

|\omega|+|am\Xi|

namely

(7.29)

\displaystyle b\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|\omega|+|am\Xi|\right)\Big|\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}\Big||v|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\left(|\omega|+|am\Xi|\right)|v|\left|g_{1}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}u\right|

\displaystyle+\left(|\omega|+|am\Xi|\right)|v|\left|2g_{1}^{\prime}H+(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right|\Big)dr^{\star}.

Now, in view of (
7.27
) inequality (
7.29
) implies the following

(7.30)

\displaystyle b\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{3}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)\right)|v|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\left(|\omega|+|am\Xi|\right)|v|\left|g_{1}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}u\right|

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}r\left|2g_{1}^{\prime}H+(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right|^{2}\Big)dr^{\star},

after appropriate Young’s inequalities on the right hand side of (
7.29
). Now, in view of the boundary conditions of (
7.4
), inequality (
7.30
) implies the desired estimate (
7.32
), for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, after appropriate Young’s inequalities.

∎

7.7.
Preparatory energy estimate for
v
for the superradiant frequencies
\mathcal{SF}

We need the following Lemma for the superradiant frequencies
\mathcal{SF}
. Note that the proof of the following Lemma
7.4
is significantly more involved than the proof of the proof of Lemma
7.3
. Specifically, the technical difficulty of non-differentiability of the function
g_{2}(\omega,m,\cdot)
, see Proposition
5.3
, is resolved with the use of quantitative non-trapping at of the superradiant frequencies, after using Lemma
7.2
for an appropriately chosen smooth function
f=f_{s}
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
and let
\epsilon>0
be sufficiently small. Let

(7.31)

(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF}.

Let
u
be a smooth solution of the inhomogeneous radial ode (
7.3
) that satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions (
7.4
). Let
v
be as in Definition
7.1
.

Then, we have

(7.32)

\displaystyle b(a,M,l)\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\frac{1}{r}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{3}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)|v|^{2}\right)dr

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{+}+\epsilon)^{\star}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)^{\star}}^{+\infty}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}+\mu^{2}_{KG}\right)|u|^{2}+\Delta|u^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}.

Proof.

The main technical difficulty is that we need to use Lemma
7.2
, for a non constant function
f_{s}(r)
, which produces ‘bad’ terms on the RHS of our inequalities. There terms however can be absorbed by using an inequality that is a quantitative manifestation of the non-trapping of superradiant frequencies, see already (
7.50
).

We note from Definition
5.2
that the following holds

(7.33)

g_{2}(r)=-\left|\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right|\sqrt{\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}}=-|am\Xi|\frac{|r_{s}^{2}-r^{2}|}{\sqrt{\Delta}}=-|am\Xi||r-r_{s}|\frac{r_{s}+r}{\sqrt{\Delta}},

where
\omega=\frac{am\Xi}{r_{s}^{2}+a^{2}}
. Note that
g_{2}(\omega,m,r)
is not differentiable in
r
at
r_{s}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
. Furthermore, recall from Proposition
5.1
that for the superradiant frequencies
(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF}
we have

r_{s}(\omega,m)=r_{crit}(\omega,m).

In the present proof we will use
r_{s}
instead of
r_{crit}
, for notational brevity.

Note that the following hold in a distributional sense

(7.34)

\displaystyle\frac{dg_{2}}{dr}(r)

\displaystyle=-\text{sign}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\cdot\frac{d}{dr}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\text{sign}\left(r-r_{s}\right)\cdot\frac{d}{dr}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}g_{2}}{dr^{2}}(r)

\displaystyle=-|am\Xi|\left(2\delta_{r_{s}}\cdot\frac{r_{s}+r}{\sqrt{\Delta}}+2\text{sign}(r-r_{s})\frac{r+r_{s}}{\sqrt{\Delta}}+|r-r_{s}|\frac{d^{2}}{dr^{2}}\left(\frac{r+r_{s}}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\right)\right),

where
\delta_{r_{s}}
is the Dirac delta centered at
r_{s}
.

Now, we note from the energy estimate of Lemma
7.2
for the superradiant frequencies that the following holds

(7.35)

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}f_{s}(r)|v|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}g_{2}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(iu\bar{v}\right)dr^{\star}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}g_{1}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}u^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}v^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right)\right)dr^{\star},

where
f_{s}
is a smooth function that we define now.

The definition of
f_{s}(\omega,m,r)

Let
\epsilon>0
be sufficiently small (see also inequality (
7.50
) which is a quantitative manifestation of that the superradiant frequencies are non trapped). Moreover, we define the following function

(7.36)

\epsilon_{s}(\omega,m)=\epsilon\cdot\min\{\left|1-\frac{r_{s}(\omega,m)}{r_{+}}\right|,\left|1-\frac{r_{s}(\omega,m)}{\bar{r}_{+}}\right|\}.

It is easy to now construct a smooth (frequency dependent) decreasing function
f_{s}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\times[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, with formula

(7.37)

f_{s}(\omega,m,r)=\begin{cases}-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)=am\Xi\frac{(r-r_{s})(r_{s}+r)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})(r_{s}^{2}+a^{2})},\quad r\in(r_{s}-\epsilon_{s}(\omega,m),r_{s}+\epsilon_{s}(\omega,m)),\\
-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)=am\Xi\frac{(r_{+}-r_{s})(r_{s}+r_{+})}{(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2})(r_{s}^{2}+a^{2})},\quad r>r_{s}+2\epsilon_{s}(\omega,m),\\
-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)=am\Xi\frac{(\bar{r}_{+}-r_{s})(r_{s}+\bar{r}_{+})}{(\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2})(r_{s}^{2}+a^{2})},\quad r<r_{s}-2\epsilon_{s}(\omega,m),\end{cases}

such that for
r\in(r_{s}-2\epsilon_{s},r_{s}-\epsilon_{s})\cup(r_{s}+\epsilon_{s},r_{s}+2\epsilon_{s})
we have that

(7.38)

|\frac{df_{s}}{dr}(\omega,m,r)|\leq B|am\Xi|,

where the constant
B
is frequency independent. Furthermore, for any
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z},r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
and for any
r_{s}(\omega,m)\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
it is easy to see from the definition of the function
f_{s}(r)
, see (
7.37
), that the inequality (
7.38
) holds.

Upper bounds for the first two terms on the RHS of (
7.35
)

Now, by recalling the definition of the tortoise coordinate
r^{\star}
, see Section
2.12
, and the distributional derivatives (
7.34
), we note that the first term on the right hand side of (
7.35
) is equal to

(7.39)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}g_{2}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(iu\bar{v}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=-\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\frac{d}{dr}g_{2}\right)f_{s}(r)\imaginary(iu\bar{v})dr

\displaystyle=-\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\frac{dg_{2}}{dr}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\frac{d^{2}g_{2}}{dr^{2}}\right)f_{s}(r)\imaginary(iu\bar{v})dr

\displaystyle=-\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\left(|am\Xi|\cdot\text{sign}\left(r-r_{s}\right)\cdot\frac{d}{dr}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\right)\right)f_{s}(r)\imaginary(iu\bar{v})dr

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|am\Xi|\left(2\delta_{r_{s}}\cdot\frac{r_{s}+r}{\sqrt{\Delta}}+2\text{sign}(r-r_{s})\frac{r+r_{s}}{\sqrt{\Delta}}+|r-r_{s}|\frac{d^{2}}{dr^{2}}\left(\frac{r+r_{s}}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\right)\right)f_{s}(r)\imaginary(iu\bar{v})dr

\displaystyle=-\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\left(|am\Xi|\cdot\text{sign}\left(r-r_{s}\right)\cdot\frac{d}{dr}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\right)\right)f_{s}(r)\imaginary(iu\bar{v})dr

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|am\Xi|\left(2\text{sign}(r-r_{s})\frac{r+r_{s}}{\sqrt{\Delta}}+|r-r_{s}|\frac{d^{2}}{dr^{2}}\left(\frac{r+r_{s}}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\right)\right)f_{s}(r)\imaginary(iu\bar{v})dr.

From (
7.39
) and from the definition of
f_{s}
, see (
7.37
), we note that the first two terms on the right hand side of (
7.35
) are bounded by

(7.40)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(g_{2}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(iu\bar{v}\right)+g_{1}^{\prime\prime}f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}u^{\prime}\right)\right|\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon B\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}}|v|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\frac{B}{\epsilon}\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{+}+\epsilon)^{\star}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}+\frac{B}{\epsilon}\int_{(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)^{\star}}^{+\infty}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{B}{\epsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)|u|^{2}+\Delta|u^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
independent of the frequencies
\omega,m,\ell
.

Upper bound for the third term on the RHS of (
7.35
)

The third term on the right hand side of (
7.35
) admits the following bound

(7.41)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}|f^{\prime}_{s}(r)\bar{v}v^{\prime}|dr^{\star}\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{3}(r^{2}+a^{2})}\left(\epsilon(am\Xi)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1_{\supp f_{s}^{\prime}}}{\epsilon}|v^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

where we used the bound (
7.38
) and appropriate Young’s inequalities.

An integrated estimate with degeneration at
r=r_{s}

By recalling the distributional derivative (
7.34
), the exam form of the function
f_{s}(r)
, see (
7.37
), and the bounds (
7.40
), (
7.41
) we obtain from (
7.35
) the following

(7.42)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|am\Xi|^{2}\left|1-\frac{r_{s}}{r}\right|\frac{1}{r}|v|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon B\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{3}}|v|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\frac{B}{\epsilon}\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{+}+\epsilon)^{\star}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}+\frac{B}{\epsilon}\int_{(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)^{\star}}^{+\infty}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{B}{\epsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)|u|^{2}+\Delta|u^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|f^{\prime}_{s}(r)\bar{v}v^{\prime}|+|am\Xi|f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right)\right)\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon B\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{3}}|v|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\frac{B}{\epsilon}\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{+}+\epsilon)^{\star}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}+\frac{B}{\epsilon}\int_{(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)^{\star}}^{+\infty}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{B}{\epsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)|u|^{2}+\Delta|u^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{3}(r^{2}+a^{2})}\left(\epsilon(am\Xi)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1_{\supp f_{s}^{\prime}}}{\epsilon}|v^{\prime}|^{2}\right)+f_{s}(r)\imaginary\left(\bar{v}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right)\right)\right)dr^{\star}.

Now, we want to estimate the term

(7.43)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{3}(r^{2}+a^{2})}\frac{1_{\supp f_{s}^{\prime}}}{\epsilon}|v^{\prime}|^{2}dr^{\star}

of the right hand side of (
7.42
) and more importantly
‘cover’ the degeneration at
r_{s}
on the left hand side of (
7.42
), which as discussed will be achieved by using the
quantitative manifestation
that the superradiant frequencies are non trapped, see already inequality (
7.50
). We proceed as follows.

The use of quantitative non trapping of superradiant frequencies

Let
\tilde{h}_{s}(\omega,m,r)\geq 0
be a smooth function in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
such that
\supp(\tilde{h}_{s}(\omega,m,\cdot))=\supp(f_{s}^{\prime}(\omega,m,\cdot))
, where
\tilde{h}_{s}(\omega,m,\cdot)=0
only at the boundary
\partial(\supp(\tilde{h}_{s}))
and
\tilde{h}_{s}(\omega,m,r)>0
for any point
r\in(\partial\supp(f^{\prime}_{s}(\omega,m,\cdot)))^{o}
. Then, we define the function

(7.44)

h_{s}(\omega,m,r)=\frac{1}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\tilde{h}_{s}(\omega,m,r).

We use a current of the form
Q^{h_{s}}
, see Definition
3.3
, to the equation that
v
satisfies, see Lemma
7.1
, in view of that
v
is a
C^{0}(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
, the derivative
v^{\prime}
is piecewise
C^{1}(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
, see Lemma
7.1
and the RHS of (
7.9
) of Lemma
7.1
is a distribution. We here note that in our companion
[
28
]
the multipliers that were used in the current
Q^{h}
also depend on the frequency
\ell
, but this is not the case in the present paper. We obtain

(7.45)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(h_{s}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(h_{s}(V-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h_{s}^{\prime\prime}\right)|v|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=Q^{h_{s}}[v](\infty)+Q^{h_{s}}(r_{s}+)-Q^{h_{s}}(r_{s}-)-Q^{h_{s}}[v](-\infty)

\displaystyle-\real\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigg(h_{s}v\left(-2i\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}\bar{v}\right)+h_{s}v\left(g_{1}^{\prime\prime}\bar{u}^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}\bar{u}+\frac{\bar{v}}{g_{1}}(V_{\textit{SL}}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}})^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+vh_{s}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}\bar{H}+\overline{\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H}\right)\Bigg)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=[Q^{h_{s}}[v]]_{r_{s}-}^{r_{s}+}-\real\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigg(vh_{s}\left(g_{1}^{\prime\prime}\bar{u}^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}\bar{u}+\frac{\bar{v}}{g_{1}}(V_{\textit{SL}}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}})^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+vh_{s}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}\bar{H}+\overline{\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H}\right)\Bigg)dr^{\star}

where we used that

(7.46)

\real\left(i\frac{g_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}}|v|^{2}\right)=0,\qquad Q^{h_{s}}[v](+\infty)=0,\qquad Q^{h_{s}}[v](-\infty)=0,

where that last two identities are proved in view of that
v|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}=v|_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}=0
, see Section
7.3
.

In view of the definition of the current
Q^{h}
, see Definition
3.3
, we have that the boundary terms at the RHS of (
7.45
) can be bounded as follows

(7.47)

\displaystyle]_{(r_{s}-)^{\star}}^{(r_{s}+)^{\star}}

\displaystyle=[h_{s}g_{2}^{\prime}g_{1}\real\left(iu\bar{u}^{\prime}\right)]_{(r_{s}-)^{\star}}^{(r_{s}+)^{\star}}=-[h_{s}g_{2}^{\prime}g_{1}\imaginary\left(u\bar{u}^{\prime}\right)]_{(r_{s}-)^{\star}}^{(r_{s}+)^{\star}}=-[h_{s}g_{2}^{\prime}g_{1}\imaginary\left(u\bar{u}^{\prime}\right)]_{(r_{s}-)^{\star}}^{(r_{s}+)^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq B\left((\omega^{2}+(am)^{2})|u|^{2}(-\infty)+(\omega^{2}+(am)^{2})|u|^{2}(+\infty)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}(|\omega|+|am|)|u\bar{H}|dr^{\star}\right),

where we used the two identities
\imaginary[u\bar{u}^{\prime}]_{-\infty}^{(r_{s}-)^{\star}}=\imaginary\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{s})^{\star}}u\bar{H}
and
\imaginary[u\bar{u}^{\prime}]_{(r_{s}+)^{\star}}^{(+\infty}=\imaginary\int_{(r_{s}+)^{\star}}^{+\infty}u\bar{H}
.

Now, we find a lower bound for the second integrand term on the left hand side of inequality (
7.45
). Specifically, note that

(7.48)

V(r_{s})-\omega^{2}=V_{\textit{SL}}(r_{s})+V_{\textit{KG}}(r_{s})+V_{0}(r_{s})-\omega^{2}=V_{\textit{SL}}(r_{s})+V_{\textit{KG}}(r_{s})+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}(r=r_{s})\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right),

where for
V_{0},V_{\textit{SL}}
see Section
3
, and therefore for a sufficiently small positive constant
\epsilon>0
, we have

(7.49)

\epsilon_{s}(\omega,m)=\epsilon\cdot\min\{\left|1-\frac{r_{s}}{r_{+}}\right|,\left|1-\frac{r_{s}}{\bar{r}_{+}}\right|\}

such that

(7.50)

V_{0}(r)-\omega^{2}\geq b\Delta\tilde{\lambda},\qquad r\in(r_{s}-2\epsilon_{s},r_{s}+2\epsilon_{s}).

Note that inequality (
7.50
)
is a quantitative manifestation that the superradiant frequencies are non-trapped
, see
[
28
]
as well.

Therefore, (
7.45
) implies the following

(7.51)

\displaystyle b\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{s}\cdot|v^{\prime}|^{2}dr^{\star}+b\int_{\left((r_{s}-2\epsilon_{s})^{\star},(r_{s}+2\epsilon_{s})^{\star}\right)}h_{s}\cdot|am\Xi|^{2}|v|^{2}dr^{\star}+b\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{s}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)|v|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\frac{1}{2}h_{s}^{\prime\prime}||v|^{2}dr^{\star}-\real\int_{\mathbb{R}}vh_{s}\left(g_{1}^{\prime\prime}\bar{u}^{\prime}+g_{2}^{\prime\prime}i\bar{u}+\frac{\bar{v}}{g_{1}}\left(V_{\textit{SL}}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}\right)^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+(\omega^{2}+m^{2})|u|^{2}(-\infty)+(\omega^{2}+m^{2})|u|^{2}(+\infty)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}(|\omega|+|m|)|u\bar{H}|dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\real\int_{\mathbb{R}}vh_{s}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}\bar{H}+\overline{\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H}\right)dr^{\star}.

For the third integral term on the right hand side of (
7.51
) we note that

(7.52)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{s}\cdot g_{2}^{\prime\prime}\real\left(i\bar{u}v\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(h_{s}\cdot g_{2}^{\prime\prime}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}v)dr^{\star}=\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r}\frac{1}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\tilde{h}_{s}\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\frac{d}{dr}g_{2}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}v)dr

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r}\frac{1}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\tilde{h}_{s}\cdot\left(\frac{d}{dr}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\cdot\frac{dg_{2}}{dr}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\frac{d^{2}g_{2}}{dr^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}v)dr,

where we used
h_{s}=\frac{1}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\tilde{h}_{s}
.

By recalling the distributional derivative (
7.34
) of
g_{2}
, equation (
7.52
) implies the following

(7.53)

\displaystyle\text{RHS of }\eqref{eq: lem: sec: exp decay, subsec: energy estimate, lem 1, energy estimate, eq 6}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\tilde{h}_{s}|am\Xi\bar{u}v|dr+|am\Xi|\frac{\tilde{h}_{s}}{r}\Delta\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{r_{s}+r}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\imaginary(\bar{u}v)\Big|_{r^{\star}=r_{s}^{\star}}

\displaystyle=B\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\tilde{h}_{s}|am\Xi\bar{u}v|dr+|am\Xi|\frac{\tilde{h}_{s}}{r}\Delta\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{r_{s}+r}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\imaginary(v\bar{u})\Big|_{r^{\star}=r^{\star}_{s}}

\displaystyle=B\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\tilde{h}_{s}|am\Xi\bar{u}v|dr+2\cdot\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\right)(r_{s})|am\Xi|\imaginary(v\bar{u})\Big|_{r^{\star}=r^{\star}_{s}}

\displaystyle=B\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}|am\Xi\bar{u}v|dr

\displaystyle+B\left(2|am\Xi|\imaginary\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\cdot v\bar{u}\right)\Big|_{r^{\star}=-\infty}+2\imaginary\int_{-\infty}^{r_{s}^{\star}}|am\Xi|\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}v\bar{u}\right)dr^{\star}\right),

where we used the fundamental theorem of calculus for the function

(7.54)

\imaginary\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\cdot v\bar{u}\right).

Therefore, in view of that

(7.55)

\imaginary\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\cdot v\bar{u}\right)(r^{\star}=-\infty)=0

then (
7.53
) implies the following

(7.56)

\displaystyle\text{RHS of }\eqref{eq: lem: sec: exp decay, subsec: energy estimate, lem 1, energy estimate, eq 6}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}|am\Xi\bar{u}v|dr

\displaystyle+2|am\Xi|\cdot\imaginary\int_{-\infty}^{r_{s}^{\star}}\Bigg(\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\right)^{\prime}v\bar{u}+\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\bar{u}^{\prime}v

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\bar{u}\Big(g_{1}^{\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime}u+ig_{2}u^{\prime}+g_{1}u^{\prime\prime}\Big)\Bigg)dr^{\star},

\displaystyle\leq\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}|am\Xi\bar{u}v|dr

\displaystyle+2|am\Xi|\cdot\imaginary\int_{-\infty}^{r_{s}^{\star}}\Bigg(\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\right)^{\prime}v\bar{u}+\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\bar{u}^{\prime}v

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{\Delta}\cdot\tilde{h}_{s}}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}\bar{u}\left(g_{1}^{\prime}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime}u+ig_{2}u^{\prime}+g_{1}H\bar{u}\right)\Bigg)dr^{\star},

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{3}}\left(\epsilon(am\Xi)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(am\Xi)^{2}|u|^{2}+(am\Xi)^{2}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}+|am\Xi|^{2}|u|^{2}+|g_{1}H|^{2}\right)dr.

Therefore, in view of (
7.52
), (
7.56
), we note that inequality (
7.51
) implies that

(7.57)

\displaystyle b\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{s}|v^{\prime}|^{2}dr^{\star}+b\int_{\left((r_{s}-2\epsilon_{s})^{\star},(r_{s}+2\epsilon_{s})^{\star}\right)}h_{s}|am\Xi|^{2}|v|^{2}dr^{\star}+b\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{s}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)|v|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\frac{1}{2}h_{s}^{\prime\prime}||v|^{2}dr^{\star}-\real\int_{\mathbb{R}}vh_{s}\left(g_{1}^{\prime\prime}\bar{u}^{\prime}+\frac{\bar{v}}{g_{1}}\left(V_{\textit{SL}}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}\right)^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{3}}\left(\epsilon(am\Xi)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(am\Xi)^{2}|u|^{2}+(am\Xi)^{2}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}+|am\Xi|^{2}|u|^{2}+|g_{1}H|^{2}\right)dr

\displaystyle+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|H|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\real\int_{\mathbb{R}}vh_{s}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}\bar{H}+\overline{\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H}\right)dr^{\star},

where recall
h_{s}=\frac{1}{r^{3}(r^{2}+a^{2})}\tilde{h}_{s}
.

By combining inequalities (
7.42
) and (
7.57
) we conclude

(7.58)

\displaystyle b\int_{\mathbb{R}}|am\Xi|^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\left|1-\frac{r_{s}}{r}\right|\frac{1}{r}\right)|v|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{+}+\epsilon)^{\star}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)^{\star}}^{+\infty}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)|u|^{2}+\Delta|u^{\prime}|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, after appropriate Young’s inequalities. For the superradiant frequencies

(7.59)

(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF}

the following inequality holds uniformly

(7.60)

\frac{\Delta}{r^{3}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{r}\left|\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right|^{2}\lesssim|am\Xi|^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\left|1-\frac{r_{s}}{r}\right|\frac{1}{r}\right).

Therefore, we control the following term on the left hand side of (
7.58
)

(7.61)

\displaystyle b\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{3}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{+}+\epsilon)^{\star}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)^{\star}}^{+\infty}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)|u|^{2}+\Delta|u^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

for any sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, which concludes the proof.
∎

7.8.
Preparatory energy estimate for
v
for all
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}

Now, we prove our main estimate for
v
. Moreover, we prove a Poincare type inequality, see Lemma
7.5
, which we use to prove that at the low order there is no loss of derivatives at trapping.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Moreover, let
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}
. Then, for any sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
we have the following.

Let
u
be a smooth solution of the inhomogeneous radial ode (
7.3
) that satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions (
7.4
). Let
v
be as in Definition
7.1
. Then, the following energy estimate holds

(7.62)

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}(r-r_{+})^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}(\bar{r}_{+}-r)^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}}{r^{3}}|v|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|v^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{+}+\epsilon)^{\star}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}+B\int_{(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)^{\star}}^{+\infty}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}+\mu^{2}_{KG}\right)|u|^{2}+\Delta|u^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

where
\tilde{\lambda}=\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+(a\omega)^{2}
, see Definition
3.2
, and for
\omega_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\penalty\ \bar{\omega}_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
.

Proof.

First, we easily combine Lemmata
7.3
,
7.4
to obtain that for any sufficiently small
\epsilon
and all
(\omega,m)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z},\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}
the following holds

(7.63)

\displaystyle b\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\frac{1}{r}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{3}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)|v|^{2}\right)dr

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{+}+\epsilon)^{\star}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)^{\star}}^{+\infty}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}
