\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}+\mu^{2}_{KG}\right)|u|^{2}+\Delta|u^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

where
\omega_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\penalty\ \bar{\omega}_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
.

Specifically, we have from (
7.63
) the following

(7.64)

\displaystyle b\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\frac{1}{r}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}dr\leq

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{+}+\epsilon)^{\star}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)^{\star}}^{+\infty}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}+\mu^{2}_{KG}\right)|u|^{2}+\Delta|u^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

for any sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
.

Now, we multiply the equation that is satisfied by
v
namely (
7.9
) with
\frac{1}{r}\bar{v}
and then take real parts. We obtain

(7.65)

\displaystyle\real\left(\frac{1}{r}\bar{v}v^{\prime\prime}\right)+\left(-\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)+\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\frac{1}{r}|v|^{2}

\displaystyle=\real\left(\frac{1}{r}\bar{v}ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}u\right)+\real\left(\frac{1}{r}\bar{v}g_{1}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime}\right)+|v|^{2}\frac{1}{g_{1}r}\left(V_{\textit{SL}}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}\right)^{\prime}+\real\left(\frac{1}{r}\bar{v}\left(2g_{1}^{\prime}H+\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right)\right).

Then, we integrate (
7.65
) and after appropriate integration by parts and rearranging we obtain

(7.66)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{r}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\frac{1}{r}\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)|v|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{r}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}dr^{\star}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{r}\left(\real\left(g_{1}^{\prime\prime}\bar{v}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}\bar{v}u\right)+|v|^{2}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\left(V_{\textit{SL}}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}\right)^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\real\left(\bar{v}v^{\prime}\right)dr^{\star}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{r}\left(\bar{v}2g_{1}^{\prime}H+\bar{v}\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right)dr^{\star}.

We note that for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
we obtain

(7.67)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{r}|v^{\prime}|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{r^{\star}(r_{+}+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{r}\left|v^{\prime}-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)v+i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)v\right|^{2}dr^{\star}+\int_{r^{\star}(r_{+}+\epsilon)}^{r^{\star}(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)}\frac{1}{r}|v^{\prime}|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{r^{\star}(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r}\left|v^{\prime}+i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)v-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)v\right|^{2}dr^{\star}.

By appropriate Young’s inequalities on (
7.67
), and in view of that that
u
satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions:

(7.68)

\displaystyle\frac{du}{dr^{\star}}=-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)u,\qquad\frac{du}{dr^{\star}}=i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)u,

at
r^{\star}=-\infty,r^{\star}=+\infty
respectively, we obtain

(7.69)

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{r^{\star}(r_{+}+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{r}\left|v^{\prime}-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)v\right|^{2}dr^{\star}+\int_{r^{\star}(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r}\left|v^{\prime}+i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)v\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{r}\left(|v^{\prime}|+\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}.

Finally, to control the term

(7.70)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{r}\real\left(g_{1}^{\prime\prime}\bar{v}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}^{\prime\prime}\bar{v}u\right)dr^{\star}

on the right hand side of (
7.66
), we use an estimate similar to the estimate (
7.40
) of Lemma
7.4
which recall controls a similar term.

Therefore, we combine (
7.63
), (
7.66
), (
7.67
), (
7.69
) and conclude the result of the Proposition after using appropriate Young’s inequalities.
∎

We need the following Lemma, whose estimate is a Poincare type inequality.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. For any
\epsilon_{p},\epsilon>0
sufficiently small we have the following. Let
u
be a smooth function in
\mathbb{R}
, that satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions (
7.4
) (not necessarily a solution of Carter’s radial ode (
7.3
)). Let
v
be as in Definition
7.1
.

Then, the following holds

(7.71)

\displaystyle b\frac{1}{\epsilon_{p}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon_{p}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right|\frac{\left|r-r_{\textit{trap}}\right|}{r^{4}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon_{p}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{r-r_{\textit{trap}}}{r^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})u|^{2}dr.

Proof.

We note

(7.72)

\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}dr^{\star}=\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{d}{dr}(r-r_{\textit{trap}})|u|^{2}dr,

where for trapping parameter
r_{\textit{trap}}(\omega,m,\ell)
see Theorem
4.1
. We integrate by parts, equation (
7.72
), to obtain

(7.73)

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\Bigg(2\frac{1}{r^{4}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)(r-r_{\textit{trap}})|u|^{2}-\frac{4}{r^{5}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}(r-r_{\textit{trap}})|u|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{(r-r_{\textit{trap}})}{r^{4}}\left(\bar{u}\frac{d}{dr}u+u\frac{d}{dr}\bar{u}\right)\Bigg)dr

\displaystyle=-\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\Bigg(2\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\frac{(r-r_{\textit{trap}})}{r^{4}}|u|^{2}-4\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{(r-r_{\textit{trap}})}{r^{5}}|u|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{g_{1}(r^{\star})}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\right)^{2}\frac{(r-r_{\textit{trap}})}{r^{4}}\left(\bar{u}g_{1}\frac{d}{dr}u+ug_{1}\frac{d}{dr}\bar{u}\right)\Bigg)dr

\displaystyle=-\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\Big(2\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\frac{(r-r_{\textit{trap}})}{r^{4}}|u|^{2}-4\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{(r-r_{\textit{trap}})}{r^{5}}|u|^{2}\Big)dr

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{(r-r_{\textit{trap}})}{r^{4}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left(\bar{u}\left(g_{1}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}(\omega,m,r^{\star})u\right)+u\overline{\left(g_{1}u^{\prime}+ig_{2}(\omega,m,r^{\star})u\right)}\right)dr.

We finally use Young’s inequality to obtain

(7.74)

\displaystyle b\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon_{p}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right|\frac{\left|r-r_{\textit{trap}}\right|}{r^{4}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon_{p}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{r-r_{\textit{trap}}}{r^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})u|^{2}dr.

We conclude the following

(7.75)

\displaystyle b\frac{1}{\epsilon_{p}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon_{p}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right|\frac{\left|r-r_{\textit{trap}}\right|}{r^{4}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon_{p}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{r-r_{\textit{trap}}}{r^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}|(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})u|^{2}dr,

and the Lemma.
∎

7.9.
The proof of Theorem
7.1

Proof.

First, we recall the energy estimate of Proposition
7.1

(7.76)

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}(r-r_{+})^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}(\bar{r}_{+}-r)^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}}{r^{3}}|v|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|v^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr

\displaystyle\leq B\Bigg(\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{+}+\epsilon)^{\star}}\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}+\int_{(\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon)^{\star}}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u\right|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega^{2}+\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}+\mu^{2}_{KG}\right)|u|^{2}+\Delta|u^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+B\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+B\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, where
\tilde{\lambda}=\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+(a\omega)^{2}
, see Definition
3.2
.

Now, we estimate the first bulk term on the right hand side of (
7.76
) by using the result of the Poincare type estimate of Lemma
7.5
for any sufficiently small
\epsilon_{p}>0
to obtain

(7.77)

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}(r-r_{+})^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}(\bar{r}_{+}-r)^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}}{r^{3}}|v|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|v^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr+\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}}\frac{1}{r^{4}}\omega^{2}1_{\{(\omega,m,\tilde{\lambda}):r_{trap}\neq 0\}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq B\Bigg(\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}-(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(1_{\{|m|>0\}}|u|^{2}+\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}(\omega,m,\tilde{\lambda})}{r}\right)^{2}(\omega+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell})|u|^{2}\right)dr\Bigg)

\displaystyle+B\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+B\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

which is the desired result. Note that to use the Poincare estimate of Lemma
7.5
to obtain (
7.77
) we used that the LHS of (
7.76
) in conjunction with the LHS of Theorem
4.1
control the following

(7.78)

\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\Delta\left(\omega^{2}+a^{2}m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}dr.

We conclude.
∎

8.
Proof of Theorem
1

The main ingredients in the proof of Theorem
1
is the fixed frequency Theorem
7.1
in conjunction with the Morawetz estimate of Theorem
4.1
, proved in our companion
[
28
]
, and several pseudodifferential commutation arguments on the Kerr–de Sitter manifold, see Section
B
.

8.1.
Assumption on the smoothness of
\psi

Let
T\geq 3
and let

(8.1)

0\leq\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{1}+T.

In view of general density arguments, in the present Section, it suffices to make the assumption that the solution
\psi
of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
)

(8.2)

\textit{arises\penalty\ from\penalty\ smooth\penalty\ initial\penalty\ data\penalty\ on\penalty\ the\penalty\ hypersurface\penalty\ $\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}-\tilde{T}\}$}.

This assumption will allow us to use the fixed frequency energy estimates of Theorem
7.1
(which recall refer to smooth solutions of Carter’s radial ode satisfying outgoing boundary conditions), in view of Carter’s Proposition
3.1
.

8.2.
The constants and bad behavior in the limit
a\rightarrow 0

We use the constants

(8.3)

b(a,M,l,\mu_{\textit{KG}})>0,\qquad B(a,M,l,\mu_{\textit{KG}})>0

with the algebra of constants

(8.4)

b+b=b,\qquad b\cdot b=b,\qquad B+B=B,\qquad B\cdot B=B.

Starting from Section
8.7
the constant
b
will degenerate in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
, while the constant
B
will blow up in the same limit. Note, however, that the constants
b,B
are finite and non zero for the Schwarzschild–de Sitter case
a=0
. This bad behavior of our constants stems from the commutation estimates and specifically from the bahavior of the symbol
\widetilde{g}_{2}
, see Lemma
5.1
.

8.3.
Frequency localization

Let
\psi
be the smooth solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
) in the domain
D(\tau_{1}-\tilde{T},\tau_{2})
where
\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}+T^{2}
, arising from the initial data (
8.2
). We define

(8.5)

\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}=\eta^{(T)}_{\tau_{1}}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}\psi\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi,

where for the smooth cut-offs
\eta^{(T)}_{\tau_{1}},\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}
see Section
2.11
.

In view of Proposition
3.1
we frequency localize the function (
8.5
) and obtain

(8.6)

u^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell},\qquad\Psi=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}

and note that the radial function
u
solves the following radial ode

(8.7)

u^{\prime\prime}+\left(\omega^{2}-V\right)u=H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}

for all
\omega,m,\ell
, with

(8.8)

H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left(\rho^{2}F\right)^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell},\qquad F\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu(\Box\eta)\cdot\left(\chi_{+}^{2}\cdot\psi\right)+2\nabla^{a}\eta\nabla_{a}\left(\chi_{+}^{2}\cdot\psi\right).

8.4.
Asymptotic behavior

We note from Carter’s Proposition
3.1
that for
u
as in (
8.6
) then for all
\omega\in\mathbb{R},m\in\mathbb{Z},\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}
the following outgoing boundary conditions hold

(8.9)

\displaystyle u^{\prime}=-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)u,\qquad u^{\prime}=i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)u,

in a limiting sense, at
r^{\star}=-\infty
,
r^{\star}=\infty
respectively.

8.5.
The function
v
and its asymptotic behavior

Let
u^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}
be as in Section
8.3
. Then, in accordance to Definition
7.1
of the previous Section, in the present Section we define

(8.10)

v=\left(g_{1}\partial_{r^{\star}}+ig_{2}(\omega,m,r)\right)u,

where for
g_{1},g_{2}
see Definition
5.2
.

In view of the assumptions of Section
8.1
and the asymptotic behavior of
u
, see Section
8.4
, we use the definition of
g_{1},g_{2}
, see Definition
5.2
, to conclude

(8.11)

\lim_{r^{\star}\rightarrow\pm\infty}v=0.

8.6.
The control of the derivatives of
\mathcal{G}\left(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\right)

The following is an energy estimate for
\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)
.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
1
hold. Moreover, assume that
\psi
arises from smooth initial data, in accordance to the assumptions of Section
8.1
.

Then, we obtain the following estimate

(8.12)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}dg\left(\frac{\Delta}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+1_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\Big(\Delta^{-1}\left|\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\rho^{2}F\right|^{2}+r\left|\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\rho^{2}F\right)\right|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2},

for any
\tau_{1}\geq 0
, where for the error term
F
see (
8.8
). For the cut-offs
\eta,\chi_{+}
see (
8.5
). For the regular vector field
Z^{\star}
see Section
2.5
. Also recall
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
.

Proof.

In view of the assumptions on
\psi
then we recall from Carter’s Proposition
3.1
that for any
\omega\in\mathbb{R},m\in\mathbb{Z},\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}
the frequency localized radial function

(8.13)

u^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(r),

is smooth and satisfies the outgoing boundary conditions (
8.9
).

Therefore, we can use fixed frequency Theorem
7.1
to obtain

(8.14)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\Bigg(\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}(r-r_{+})^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}(\bar{r}_{+}-r)^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}}{r^{3}}|v|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|v^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m\ell}\Bigg(\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}-(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(1_{\{|m|>0\}}m^{2}|u|^{2}+\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}(\omega,m,\ell)}{r}\right)^{2}(\omega+\tilde{\lambda})|u|^{2}\right)dr\Bigg)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\Delta^{-1}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

for a sufficienty small
\epsilon>0
, where we integrated
\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}
. For the cut-off term
H
see (
8.8
).

We use the Parseval identities of Section
5.6
to rewrite the top order terms of the LHS of (
8.14
) as follows

(8.15)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\Bigg(\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}(r-r_{+})^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}(\bar{r}_{+}-r)^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}}{r^{3}}|v|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|v^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Bigg(\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}(r-r_{+})^{-1}|\big(\partial_{r^{\star}}-(\partial_{t}+\omega_{+}\partial_{\varphi})\big)\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}(r-\bar{r}_{+})^{-1}|\big(\partial_{r^{\star}}+(\partial_{t}+\bar{\omega}_{+}\partial_{\varphi})\big)\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{r^{3}}\left|{}^{d\sigma}\nabla\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}\left|\left(\partial_{t}-\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}\right)\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r}\left|\partial_{r^{\star}}\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}\Bigg).

Moreover, in view of the Parseval identities of Section
3.4
we rewrite the horizon terms of the RHS of (
8.14
) as follows

(8.16)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle=\lim_{r^{\star}\rightarrow-\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(|\partial_{t}\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+|\partial_{\varphi}\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\lim_{r^{\star}\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(|\partial_{t}\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+|\partial_{\varphi}\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}\right)

which note can be bounded by

(8.17)

\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap D(\tau_{1},\infty)}J^{n}[\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}\cap D(\tau_{1},\infty)}J^{n}[\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}.

Therefore, in view of (
8.16
), (
8.17
), we note that the lower order terms on the RHS of (
8.15
) can be controlled from the Morawetz estimate of Theorem
4.1
. Therefore, in view of the Parseval identity (
8.15
) we obtain from (
8.14
) the following

(8.18)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}dg\left(\frac{\Delta}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}\right)

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\left(\Delta^{-1}|H|^{2}+r\left|(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right|^{2}\right)+B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2},

where we used that
\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}=\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi
, see (
8.5
).

Furthermore, it is easy to conclude the following

(8.19)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\geq b\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\left|\partial_{t^{\star}}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}\right)=\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\left|\partial_{t^{\star}}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2})\psi+\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\partial_{t^{\star}}\psi\right|^{2}+|\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\not{\nabla}(\psi)|^{2}\right)

and therefore, by using that
|\partial_{t^{\star}}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2})|\lesssim T^{-1}
in conjunction with the boundedness estimate of Theorem
4.1
we conclude that

(8.20)

\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2})^{2}\left(|\partial_{t^{\star}}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}\right)\leq B\int_{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}

which, in view of the support of the functions
\eta,\chi_{+}
and by using the boundedness estimate of Theorem
4.1
one more time, we obtain

(8.21)

\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}\left(|\partial_{t^{\star}}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}\right)\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}.

Therefore, by using the above (
8.21
) and (
8.18
), in conjunction with the Morawetz estimate of Theorem
4.1
(in order to obtain the derivatives
(Z^{\star}\psi)^{2}
in the desired domain) we obtain the following

(8.22)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}dg\left(\frac{\Delta}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+1_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\right)

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\left(\Delta^{-1}|H|^{2}+r\left|(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right|^{2}\right)+B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}.

Finally, in view of the Parseval identities of Sections
3.4
,
5.6
we have the following identity

(8.23)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\left(\Delta^{-1}|H|^{2}+r\left|(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2})H\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\Big(\Delta^{-1}\left|\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\rho^{2}F\right|^{2}+r\left|\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\rho^{2}F\right)\right|^{2}\Big),

where
\rho^{2}=r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta
and for
F
see (
8.8
). We conclude.
∎

8.7.
Bounding the inhomogeneities of Proposition
8.1

We need the following preparatory proposition whose purpose is to bound the inhomogeneous
H
terms on the RHS of Proposition
8.1
, where for
H
see (
8.8
). We will appeal to the pseudodifferential estimates of Lemma
B.1
.

Starting from the estimate (
8.31
) the constants
b,B
will respectively degenerate and blow up in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
, as discussed in Section
8.2
. Specifically, the constant
b
of estimate (
8.24
) of Proposition
8.2
degenerates in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
but, as discussed in Section
8.2
, the constants do not degenerate exactly on the Schwarzschild–de Sitter case
a=0
. This is related to the pseudodifferential commutations we need to employ, specifically see the properties of the symbol
\widetilde{g}_{2}
in Lemma
5.1
.

Proposition 8.2
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
1
hold. Moreover, assume that
\psi
is in accordance to the assumptions of Section
8.1
, specifically
\psi
arises from smooth initial data. Then, we obtain the following estimate

(8.24)

\displaystyle b\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+1_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\left(J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu},

for any
T\geq 3
and for any
\tilde{T}<\tau_{1}<\tau_{1}+T<\tau_{2}
. For the cut-offs
\eta,\chi_{+}
see (
8.5
). For the regular vector field
Z^{\star}
see Section
2.5
, where
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
.

Proof.

Since the present Proposition satisfies the same assumptions as Proposition
8.1
, we immediately have the result (
8.12
) of Proposition
8.1
, which we recall here:

(8.25)

\displaystyle b\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+1_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\Big(\Delta^{-1}\left|\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\rho^{2}F\right|^{2}+r\left|\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\rho^{2}F\right)\right|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\left(J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}\right),

where for the error terms
H
see (
8.8
). In what follows we seek to appropriately bound the

error terms on the RHS of (
8.25
).

An auxilliary estimate

We will only use the estimate (
8.26
) of the following Lemma to prove inequalities (
8.37
), (
8.47
).

Lemma 8.1
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
1
hold. Moreover, assume that
\psi
is in accordance to the assumptions of Section
8.1
, specifically
\psi
arises from smooth initial data. Then, we obtain the following estimate

(8.26)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B}{\epsilon_{p}}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+B\epsilon_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left|\partial_{t}\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\epsilon_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left|a\Xi\partial_{\varphi}\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}.

for any sufficiently small
\epsilon_{p}>0
.

Proof.

The assumptions of the present Lemma allow us access to the results of Theorem
4.1
, specifically the Morawetz and boundedness estimates. Next, we use the Poincare type Lemma
7.5
in order to ‘cover’ the lower order degeneratation at
r_{trap}(\omega,m,\ell)
at the LHS of the Morawetz estimate, at the expence of introducing an appropriate top order term on the RHS. Specifically, we obtain the following estimate

(8.27)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m,\ell}\Bigg(\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}-(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(1_{\{|m|>0\}}|u|^{2}+\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell})|u|^{2}\right)dr\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B}{\epsilon_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m,\ell}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(1-\frac{r_{trap}}{r}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}+B\epsilon_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}dr\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\omega^{2}+(am\Xi)^{2})|v|^{2}.

Recall from Section
8.5
that
v=(g_{1}\partial_{r^{\star}}+ig_{2})u
, and
u
is the frequency localization of
\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi
, see Section
8.3
.

Therefore, by using the Morawetz estimate of Theorem
4.1
one last time to bound the first term on the RHS of the above (
8.27
) we obtain

(8.28)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m,\ell}\Bigg(\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}-(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(1_{\{|m|>0\}}|u|^{2}+\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell})|u|^{2}\right)dr\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B}{\epsilon_{p}}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+B\epsilon_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}dr\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\omega^{2}+(am\Xi)^{2})|v|^{2}.

Now, we use the Parseval identities of Section
5.6
to obtain

(8.29)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}dr\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\omega^{2}+(am\Xi)^{2})|v|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left|\partial_{t}\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left|a\Xi\partial_{\varphi}\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2},

and moreover we use the Parseval identities of Section
3.4
to write the LHS of (
8.28
) in physical space and obtain the desired estimate.
∎

Upper bound for the bulk of the RHS of
(
8.25
)

To bound the bulk term on the RHS of (
8.25
) we will use the pseudodifferential commutator estimates of Lemma
B.1
multiple times.

By using the operator
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
, see Definition
5.4
and the coarea formula we estimate the bulk of the right hand side of (
8.25
) as follows

(8.30)

\displaystyle\boxed{\text{Bulk on RHS of }\eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq 0}}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}(\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\right)\right|^{2}+r\left|\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\nabla^{a}\eta\nabla_{a}\left(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\right)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\Delta^{-2}\left|\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left((\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi+2\nabla^{a}\eta\nabla_{a}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|(\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}(\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+r\left|(\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta^{-2}\left|\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left((\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi+2\nabla^{a}\eta\nabla_{a}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}.

For the cut-offs
\eta,\chi_{+}
see Section
2.11
.

Now, by using the pseudodifferential estimate (
B.3
) of Lemma
B.1
we obtain from (
8.30
) the following

(8.31)

\displaystyle\boxed{\text{Bulk on RHS of }\eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq 0}}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|(\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}(\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\right)\right|^{2}+r\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}(\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+r\left|(\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}+r\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta^{-2}\left|\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left((\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi+2\nabla^{a}\eta\nabla_{a}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\chi_{+}^{2}]\Box\eta\psi\right|^{2}+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}](\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\chi_{+}^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\Box\eta\psi)\right|^{2}+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}(\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\right)\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta^{-2}\left|\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left((\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi+2\nabla^{a}\eta\nabla_{a}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\chi_{+}^{2}]\Box\eta\psi\right|^{2}+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}](\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\Box\eta\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}-\mathcal{G}+\mathcal{G})(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}(\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\right)\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta^{-2}\left|\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left((\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi+2\nabla^{a}\eta\nabla_{a}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}.

The constants on the RHS of the above estimate blows up in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
, in view of the estimates of Lemma
B.1
. We immediately bound the right hand side of (
8.31
) as follows

(8.32)

\displaystyle\boxed{\text{Bulk on RHS of }\eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq 0}}\leq

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}](\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}-\mathcal{G})(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\chi_{+}^{2}]\Box\eta\psi\right|^{2}+r\left|\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\Box\eta\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}(\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\right)\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\Delta^{-2}\left|\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left((\Box\eta)\chi_{+}^{2}\psi+2\nabla^{a}\eta\nabla_{a}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}.

Now, by using the support of the functions
\eta,\chi
and that

(8.33)

|Z^{\star}\eta|+|\partial_{t}\eta|+|\not{\nabla}\eta|\leq\frac{B}{T},\qquad|\partial_{t^{\star}}^{2}\eta|\leq\frac{B}{T^{2}},\qquad|Z^{\star}\chi_{+}|+|\partial_{t}\chi_{+}|+|\not{\nabla}\chi_{+}|\leq\frac{B}{T}

see Section
2.11
and the
H^{1}
boundedness result

(8.34)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}|\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}|\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

of Theorem
4.1
we
bound the zero order terms
of the right hand side of (
8.32
) and obtain the following

(8.35)

\displaystyle\boxed{\text{Bulk on RHS of }\eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq 0}}\leq

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}](\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}-\mathcal{G})(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2})}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}](\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\chi^{2}_{+}\psi]+|\chi_{+}^{2}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{B}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2})}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|\left(\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}](\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2},

where in the second to last inequality of (
8.35
) we use the bound on the derivatives and the support of the cut-offs (
8.33
) and in the last inequality we use the
H^{1}
boundedness result (
8.34
).

Now, by using the bounds on the derivatives of the cut-offs (
8.33
) we obtain from (
8.35
) the following

(8.36)

\displaystyle\boxed{\text{Bulk on RHS of }\eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq 0}}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}](\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{B}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2})}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}.

Now, in view of the pseudodifferential commutator estimates of Lemma
B.1
and the coarea formula, see Section
2.9
, we bound the commutation terms of (
8.36
) as follows

(8.37)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}r\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\frac{\Delta\rho^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}g^{ab}\partial_{a}\eta\nabla_{b}](\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}J^{n}_{\mu}[\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B}{\epsilon_{p}}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\epsilon_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left|\partial_{t}\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\epsilon_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left|a\Xi\partial_{\varphi}\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}.

where we used the
H^{1}
boundedness result (
8.34
) and the estimate (
8.26
) of Lemma
8.1
mentioned in the beginning of the proof.

From (
8.36
) and (
8.37
) we obtain the following

(8.38)

\displaystyle\boxed{\text{Bulk on RHS of }\eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq 0}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B}{\epsilon_{p}}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\epsilon_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left|\partial_{t}\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\epsilon_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{g_{1}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left|a\Xi\partial_{\varphi}\mathcal{G}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{B}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2})}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu},

where
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
.

Putting everything together

Therefore, inequality (
8.38
) for a sufficiently small
\epsilon_{p}>0
implies

(8.39)

\displaystyle b\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}(Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi))^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}(W\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi))^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+1_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu},

for
T\geq 3
and
\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{1}+T<\tau_{1}+2T<\tau_{2}
.
∎

8.8.
Modifying the LHS of Proposition
8.2

We want to appropriately modify the LHS of the estimate of Proposition
8.2
. We will do so by appealing to the pseudifferential estimates of Lemma
B.1
.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
1
hold. Moreover, assume that
\psi
is in accordance to the assumptions of Section
8.1
, specifically
\psi
arises from smooth initial data on the hypersurface
\{t^{\star}=0\}
. Then, we obtain the following estimate

(8.40)

\displaystyle b\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}dg\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu},

for
T\geq 3
and
\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{1}+T<\tau_{1}+2T<\tau_{2}
. For the cut-offs
\eta,\chi_{+}
see (
8.5
). For the regular vector field
Z^{\star}
see Section
2.5
. Also, recall
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
.

Proof.

In Proposition
8.2
we proved, with the assumptions of Theorem
1
, that the following holds

(8.41)

\displaystyle b\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)|^{2}+1_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu},

for
T\geq 3
and
\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{1}+T<\tau_{1}+2T<\tau_{2}
, where for the cut-offs
\eta,\chi_{+}
see (
8.5
) and the Figure
10
.

We want to modify the LHS of (
8.41
) in order to appropriately display only the pseudodifferential operator

(8.42)

\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}

where we use the notation

(8.43)

\mathcal{G}_{\chi}=\chi\mathcal{G}\chi,

for a smooth function
\chi:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. Specifically, we want to prove that the following hold

(8.44)

\displaystyle b\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left|\not{\nabla}\left(\eta\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\text{LHS of}\penalty\ \eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq -1}+\text{RHS of}\penalty\ \eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq -1},

\displaystyle b\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|Z^{\star}\left(\eta\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\text{LHS of}\penalty\ \eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq -1}+\text{RHS of}\penalty\ \eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq -1},

\displaystyle b\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|W\left(\eta\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\text{LHS of}\penalty\ \eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq -1}+\text{RHS of}\penalty\ \eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq -1}.

We only prove the second estimate of (
8.44
), which is the most elaborate, and note that the proofs of the first and second inequality of (
8.44
) are similar.

Proof of the second estimate of (
8.44
)

First, we note the following

(8.45)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|Z^{\star}\left(\eta\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|Z^{\star}\left(\eta\chi_{+}\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|Z^{\star}\left(\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)+[\eta\chi_{+},\mathcal{G}](\chi_{+}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}+\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|Z^{\star}\left([\eta\chi_{+},\mathcal{G}](\chi_{+}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\text{LHS of}\penalty\ \eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq -1}+\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|Z^{\star}\left([\eta\chi_{+},\mathcal{G}](\chi_{+}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}.

Moreover, we bound the last term on the RHS of (
8.45
) as follows

(8.46)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|Z^{\star}\left([\eta\chi_{+},\mathcal{G}](\chi_{+}\psi)\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\Bigg|Z^{\star}(\eta\chi_{+})\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}\psi)-\mathcal{G}\left(Z^{\star}(\eta\chi_{+})\cdot\chi_{+}\psi\right)-[\mathcal{G},\eta\chi_{+}](Z^{\star}(\chi_{+}\psi))-[Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}](\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)+\eta\chi_{+}[Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}](\chi_{+}\psi)\Bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta(Z^{\star}(\eta\chi_{+}))^{2}(\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}\psi))^{2}+\Delta\left([\mathcal{G},Z^{\star}(\eta\chi_{+})](\chi_{+}\psi)\right)^{2}+\Delta\left(Z^{\star}(\eta\chi_{+})\cdot\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}\psi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left([\mathcal{G},\eta\chi_{+}](Z^{\star}(\chi_{+}\psi))\right)^{2}+\Delta\left([Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}](\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right)^{2}+\Delta\left(\eta\chi_{+}[Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}](\chi_{+}\psi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu},

where the first equality of (
8.46
) holds pointwise for the integrands (after a lengthy computation) and the last estimate of (
8.46
) is proved by using the pseudodifferential commutations of Lemma
B.1
and by recalling the support of the cut–off functions, see Section
2.11
and the
H^{1}
boundedness result of Theorem
4.1
.

Now we want to bound the last bulk term on the RHS of (
8.46
). We will use the estimate (
8.26
) of Lemma
8.1
, for a sufficiently small
\epsilon_{p}>0
. We immediately obtain

(8.47)

b\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}\leq\text{LHS of}\penalty\ \eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq -1}+\text{RHS of}\penalty\ \eqref{eq: proof thm 3 exp decay, eq -1}.\\

Therefore, by (
8.47
), (
8.46
) we conclude the second inequality of (
8.44
).

Putting everything together

Now, by using the now proved (
8.44
) then our main inequality (
8.41
) implies

(8.48)

\displaystyle b\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}dg\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}\Bigg)+b\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu},

where
T\geq 3
and
\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{1}+T<\tau_{1}+2T<\tau_{2}
. For the timelike vector field field
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
see Lemma
2.2
.
∎

8.9.
Modifying the RHS of Proposition
8.3

We want to appropriately modify the RHS of the estimate of Proposition
8.3
to display
\mathcal{G}(\chi_{-}\psi)
. We will do so by appealing to the pseudodifferential estimates of Lemma
B.1
.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
1
hold. Moreover, assume that
\psi
is in accordance to the assumptions of Section
8.1
, specifically
\psi
arises from smooth initial data. Then, we obtain the following estimate

(8.49)

\displaystyle b\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}dg\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi]n^{\mu},

for
T\geq 3
and
\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{1}+T<\tau_{1}+2T<\tau_{2}
. For the cut-offs
\eta,\chi_{+}
see (
8.5
). For the regular vector field
Z^{\star}
see Section
2.5
, where
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
.

Proof.

In view of Proposition
8.3
, it suffices to prove the following estimate

(8.50)

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu}\leq B\cdot T\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+B\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi]n^{\mu}d\tau^{\prime},

for
\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}
, where for the cut offs
\chi_{-},\chi_{+}
see Section
2.11
, and for their graphic representation see Figure
10
. Recall the vector field
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
.

Figure 10.
The cut-offs
\chi_{+},\chi_{-}

To prove (
8.50
) we first note that
\chi_{-}\Big|_{\{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T\}}\equiv 1
. Therefore, we write

(8.51)

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)]n^{\mu}d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}1_{\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}+T\}}\cdot\chi_{-}^{4}\cdot\Bigg(\Delta\left|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}=1}\left|W^{i_{1}}\not{\nabla}^{i_{2}}\mathcal{G}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi)\right|^{2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{W}_{\mu}[\chi_{+}^{2}\psi]+|\chi_{+}\psi|^{2}+1_{\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}+T\}}J^{W}_{\mu}[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi]n^{\mu},

where we used the Coifman–Meyer and pseudodifferential estimates of Lemma
B.1
, specifically we used the pseudodifferential estimates (
B.6
), (
B.7
), (
B.8
). Note that the cut-offed functions
\chi_{+}^{2}\psi,\chi_{-}\psi
vanish in the domain for
\{t^{\star}<0\}
and moreover belong in the space
H^{1}(\mathcal{M})
and therefore we can safely appeal to the pseudodifferential estimate of Lemma
B.1
.

Now, in view of the support of the function
\chi_{+}
, see Section
2.11
and Figure
10
, and the boundedness estimate of Theorem
4.1
we conclude from (
8.51
) that (
8.50
) holds.
∎

8.10.
Completing the proof of Theorem
1

We use the result of Proposition
8.4
and Theorem
4.1
to obtain

(8.52)

\displaystyle b\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+b\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}dg\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi]n^{\mu},

where
T\geq 3
,
T^{2}-2\textTau-1\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{1}+T\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}
.

The estimate (
8.52
) concludes the proof of Theorem
1
for the case of non zero Klein–Gordon mass
\mu^{2}_{KG}>0
.

To conclude the result of Theorem
1
we need to remove the zeroth order term
|\psi|^{2}
from the hypersurface term of the RHS of (
8.52
), for the case of the wave equation
\mu_{KG}=0
.

Recall from Section
2.13
the following Poincare–Wirtinger inequality

(8.53)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}|\psi-\underline{\psi}(\tau_{1})|^{2}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

where
\underline{\psi}(\tau)=\frac{1}{|\{t^{\star}=\tau\}|}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\psi
.

We will now make an assumption that will be removed at the end of the present proof. Specifically, we make the assumption that the mean of
\psi
at the hypersurface
\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}
vanishes, namely

(8.54)

\underline{\psi}(\tau_{1})=0.

By using the assumption (
8.54
) in (
8.52
) we conclude that we have

(8.55)

\displaystyle b\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+b\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}dg\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi-\underline{\psi}(\tau_{1})|^{2}+\frac{1}{T}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi]n^{\mu},

where we used the Poincare–Wirtinger inequality (
8.53
) on the RHS of (
8.52
), and therefore we obtain

(8.56)

\displaystyle b\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+b\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}dg\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi]n^{\mu}.

We note the following identity

(8.57)

[Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}]\psi=\chi_{-}\left([Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}](\chi_{-}\psi)-\mathcal{G}[Z^{\star},\chi_{-}]\psi\right)+Z^{\star}\chi_{-}\cdot\mathcal{G}(\chi_{-}\psi).

Similar, and in fact simpler, identities hold if we replace
Z^{\star}
with the operators
\partial_{t},\partial_{\varphi},\not{\nabla}
. Similar identities also hold if we replace
\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}
with
\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}
.

Now, by using (
8.57
) in (
8.56
) in conjunction with the Morawetz and boundedness estimates of Theorem
4.1
, to bound the terms on the RHS of (
8.57
) generated by the commutation, we obtain the following

(8.58)

\displaystyle b\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+b\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}dg\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}W\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}\left|\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}Z^{\star}\psi\right|^{2}+\left|\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}W\psi\right|^{2}+\left|\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\not{\nabla}\psi\right|^{2},

for any
\tau\geq\tau_{1}
, where note that we commuted all of the derivatives with the operators
\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{\pm}}
. Note that in (
8.58
) we used the following abuse of notation, for convenience: when we write
|\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}
we mean
\not{g}^{ab}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\partial_{a}\psi\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\partial_{b}\psi
, where
\not{g}
is the metric of the
r
sphere, see Section
2.7
.

For the case of the wave equation
\mu_{KG}=0
if
\psi
is a solution then
\psi+c
is a solution, for any constant
c\in\mathbb{C}
. Therefore, if
\psi
is a solution then so is
\psi-\underline{\psi}(\tau_{1})
. Therefore, by replacing
\psi
with
\psi-\underline{\psi}(\tau_{1})
in (
8.58
) we can see that (
8.58
) holds for any initial mean. To prove that (
8.56
) holds for any initial mean we use the energy estimate (
8.58
) and go back to (
8.56
) by using again the identity (
8.57
) in conjunction with the Morawetz and boundedness estimates of Theorem
4.1
.

Remark 8.1
.

Note that we concluded the proof of the main Theorem
1
from estimate (
8.52
), with a constant that blows up as
\mu_{KG}\rightarrow 0
, since there is a zeroth order term
|\psi|^{2}
on the RHS of (
8.52
) that we cannot remove. On the other hand, in the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
we can remove the zeroth order term from the RHS of (
8.52
), in view of the process described above. Therefore, as already mentioned in Remark
1.4
, the constant of the main Theorem
1
blows up in the limit
\mu_{KG}\rightarrow 0
, but we obtain the result of the main Theorem
1
for the wave case
\mu_{KG}=0
separately.

9.
Proof of Corollary
1.1

In this Section we prove Corollary
1.1
.

9.1.
Proof of the energy decay (
6.6
) of Corollary
1.1

The relatively non degenerate estimate of Theorem
1
implies the following

(9.1)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,+\infty)}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi,\psi)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C}{T}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi,\psi),

for any
T\geq 3
,
3\leq\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{1}+T\leq\tau_{2}
, and for any
\tau\geq\tau_{1}
, where we neglected the bulk integrand term
\frac{1}{\Delta}(W\mathcal{G}_{\chi}\psi)^{2}
on the LHS. For the cut-offs

(9.2)

\chi_{+}(t^{\star})=\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}(t^{\star}),\qquad\chi_{-}(t^{\star})=\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}(t^{\star}+\tilde{T})

see Section
2.11
, and for their graphic representation see Figure
9
.

We define the sequence

(9.3)

t_{i}=T+i\tilde{T},\qquad i=0,1,2,\dots.

In view of the relatively non degenerate estimate (
9.1
) we note that there exists a constant
C_{m}
such that for any
T
sufficiently large and for any
i=0,1,2,\dots
the following holds

(9.4)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{i}+T\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{t_{i}+T}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{i}\psi,\psi)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m}\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{i}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C_{m}}{T}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{i-1}\psi,\psi),

where

(9.5)

\chi_{i}=\chi_{\textit{data}}(z-i\tilde{T}),\qquad\chi_{\textit{data}}(t^{\star}):=\chi_{0}(t^{\star})=\chi_{0,T^{2}}(t^{\star}),

for
i=0,1,2\dots
and we denote:

(9.6)

\mathcal{G}_{i}:=\chi_{i}\mathcal{G}\chi_{i}.

Moreover, we recall the boundedness estimate of Theorem
4.1

(9.7)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=t^{\prime\prime}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq C_{b}\int_{\{t^{\star}=t^{\prime}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

for some constant
C_{b}>0
and for any
0<t^{\prime}\leq t^{\prime\prime}
.

By using the mean value theorem for integrals we note that there exists a

(9.8)

\tilde{t}_{1}\in[t_{1}+2T,t_{2}-T]

such that

(9.9)

(t_{2}-T-(t_{1}+2T))\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tilde{t}_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq\int_{t_{1}+T}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}.

By using (
9.9
) in the relatively non degenerate estimate (
9.4
), for
i=1
, we obtain the following

(9.10)

\displaystyle(t_{2}-t_{1}-3T)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tilde{t}_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq C_{m}\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C_{m}}{T}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\psi,\psi).

Now, by using the boundedness estimate (
9.7
), for
t^{\prime\prime}=t_{2},t^{\prime}=\tilde{t}_{1}
in the LHS of (
9.10
) we obtain

(9.11)

(t_{2}-t_{1}-3T)\frac{1}{C_{b}}\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq C_{m}\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C_{m}}{T}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\psi,\psi).

By taking
T>0
sufficiently large, we note
(t_{2}-t_{1}-3T)=T^{2}-5T-1>T
and therefore inequality (
9.11
) implies the following

(9.12)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq\frac{C_{m}\cdot C_{b}}{T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C_{m}\cdot C_{b}}{T^{2}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\psi,\psi).

Finally, we bound the hypersurface term on the RHS of the relatively non degenerate estimate (
9.4
), for
i=2
, by using (
9.12
), and we bound the bulk term on the RHS of (
9.4
) by using the relatively non degenereate estimate (
9.4
) for
i=1
to obtain

(9.13)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{2}+T\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{t_{2}+T}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{2}\psi,\psi)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m}\Big(\frac{C_{m}\cdot C_{b}}{T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C_{m}\cdot C_{b}}{T^{2}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\psi,\psi)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{m}}{T}\Big(C_{m}\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C_{m}}{T}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\psi,\psi)\Big).

We use the boundedness estimate (
9.7
) one final time and we obtain

(9.14)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{2}+T\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{t_{2}+T}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{2}\psi,\psi)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m}\Big(\frac{C_{m}\cdot C_{b}^{2}}{T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{0}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C_{m}\cdot C_{b}}{T^{2}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\psi,\psi)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{m}}{T}\Big(C_{m}\cdot C_{b}\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{0}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{C_{m}}{T}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\psi,\psi)\Big)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{C_{m}^{2}\cdot C_{b}^{2}}{T}+\frac{C_{m}^{2}C_{b}}{T}\right)\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{0}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\left(\frac{C_{m}^{2}\cdot C_{b}}{T^{2}}+\frac{C_{m}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\psi,\psi).

We now take
T
sufficiently large so that

(9.15)

\frac{C_{m}^{2}C_{b}^{2}}{T}+\frac{C_{m}^{2}C_{b}}{T}+\frac{C_{m}^{2}C_{b}}{T^{2}}+\frac{C_{m}^{2}}{T^{2}}<\frac{1}{20}.

Therefore, if

(9.16)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{0}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq E_{data},\qquad\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+T}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\psi,\psi)\leq E_{data}

for some constant
E_{data}
that will be determined later, then the estimate (
9.14
) implies that

(9.17)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{2}+T\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{t_{2}+T}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{2}\psi,\psi)\leq\frac{E_{data}}{2}

We inductively obtain that for any even number
i
we have that

(9.18)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{i}+T\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{t_{i}+T}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{i}\psi,\psi)\leq\frac{E_{data}}{2^{i-1}},\qquad i=2,4,6\dots

By using the relatively non degenerate estimate (
9.4
), for odd numbers
i=1,3,5,\dots
, and the established estimate (
9.18
), it is easy to extend the estimate (
9.18
) for any natural number
i
and obtain that the following holds

(9.19)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=t_{i}+T\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{t_{i}+T}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{i}\psi,\psi)\leq\frac{E_{data}}{2^{i-1}},\qquad i=1,2,3,\dots

Now, we note that we can take

(9.20)

E_{data}=C\Bigg(\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{\supp\chi_{\textit{data}}}d\tau^{\prime}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{\textit{data}}}\psi,\psi)\Bigg)

for some constant
C=C(a,M,l,\mu^{2}_{KG})
.

Therefore, we obtain the desired energy decay estimate (
6.6
) from (
9.19
) and the boundedness estimate (
9.7
). We can include
\tau\geq 0
by taking
C
larger if necessary.

9.2.
Proof of the pointwise estimate (
6.7
) of Corollary
1.1

It is convenient to use the algebra of constants already discussed in Section
7.1
. First, we will need an appropriate exponential decay energy estimate at the level of
H^{2}
. Specifically, we can commute the Klein–Gordon equation with
\partial_{t},\partial_{\varphi},N
appropriately many times obtain from (
6.6
) that the following holds

(9.21)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}[n^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}\leq Be^{-c\tau}E

where

(9.22)

E=\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}[n^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}[n^{3}\psi]n^{\mu}.

Now, for

(9.23)

\underline{\psi}(\tau)=\frac{1}{|\{t^{\star}=\tau\}|}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\psi(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)v(r,\theta)drd\theta d\varphi

it is straightforward to note that

(9.24)

\displaystyle|\partial_{\tau}\underline{\psi}(\tau)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\partial_{\tau}\frac{1}{|\{t^{\star}=\tau\}|}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\psi(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)v(r,\theta)drd\theta d\varphi\right|^{2}\leq B\left|\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\partial_{\tau}\psi(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)v(r,\theta)drd\theta d\varphi\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}(\partial_{t}\psi)^{2},

where in the last estimate we used a Hölder inequality for integrals, in view of that for any
\tau\geq 0
we have
|\{t^{\star}=\tau\}|=\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}dr\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi\sim 1
, where also note that
|\{t^{\star}=\tau\}|
is independent of
\tau
. Therefore, in view of (
9.24
) and of the exponential decay of the energy (
6.6
) we obtain

(9.25)

|\partial_{\tau}\underline{\psi}(\tau)|^{2}\leq B\Bigg(\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{\supp\chi_{\textit{data}}}d\tau^{\prime}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{\textit{data}}}\psi,\psi)\Bigg)e^{-c\tau}=B\cdot E_{0}e^{-c\tau}.

for some
c=c(a,M,l,\mu_{KG})>0
, where

(9.26)

E_{0}:=\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{\supp\chi_{\textit{data}}}d\tau^{\prime}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{\textit{data}}}\psi,\psi)\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n\psi]n^{\mu}.

Second, we prove that the function
\underline{\psi}(\tau)
converges for
\tau\rightarrow\infty
. Specifically, by using the fundamental theorem of calculus we obtain the following

(9.27)

\displaystyle|\underline{\psi}(t_{2})-\underline{\psi}(t_{1})|\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}|\partial_{\tau}\underline{\psi}(\tau)|d\tau\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}|\partial_{\tau}\underline{\psi}(\tau)|d\tau\leq B\sqrt{E_{0}}e^{-ct_{1}}

for any
t_{1}\leq t_{2}
, where in the last inequality we used the energy decay estimate (
6.6
). In view of inequality (
9.27
) we apply a Cauchy sequence limiting argument and conclude that the function
\underline{\psi}(\tau)
indeed converges pointwise with an exponential rate to a constant

(9.28)

\underline{\psi}

namely the following holds

(9.29)

|\underline{\psi}(\tau)-\underline{\psi}|\leq B\sqrt{E_{0}}e^{-c\tau}

for all
\tau\geq 0
. Moreover, from (
9.27
) and in view of that
\underline{\psi}(\tau)\rightarrow\underline{\psi}
we obtain that

(9.30)

|\underline{\psi}|\leq|\underline{\psi}(0)|+B\sqrt{E_{0}}.

Note that in the case where the Klein–Gordon mass is non zero
\mu^{2}_{KG}>0
we have

(9.31)

\underline{\psi}=0.

By the Poincare–Wirtinger inequality of Section
2.13
, we obtain

(9.32)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left|\psi-\underline{\psi}(\tau)\right|^{2}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

where in the last inequality we used the energy decay (
6.6
) and the Poincare–Wirtinger inequality of Section
2.13
.

Now, for any
s>\frac{n}{2}
we recall the classical Sobolev embedding

(9.33)

\mskip 2.0mu|\phi\mskip 2.0mu|_{L^{\infty}(I)}\lesssim\mskip 2.0mu|\phi\mskip 2.0mu|_{H^{s}(I)}.

where
I\subset R^{n}
is compact, see the book by Brezis
[
3
]
. We use the Sobolev embedding (
9.33
) for

(9.34)

\phi=\psi-\underline{\psi}(\tau),\qquad n=3,\qquad s=2,\qquad I=\{t^{\star}=\tau\}

in conjunction with (
9.24
) to obtain the following

(9.35)

\displaystyle\sup_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left|\psi-\underline{\psi}(\tau)\right|^{2}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n^{2}\psi]n^{\mu},

where we also used the Poincare–Wirtinger (
9.32
).

Now, we note that

(9.36)

\displaystyle\sup_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left|\psi-\underline{\psi}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\left(\sup_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left|\psi-\underline{\psi}(\tau)\right|^{2}+\sup_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left|\underline{\psi}(\tau)-\underline{\psi}\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left(J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}\right)+BE_{0}e^{-c\tau}

where in the last inequality we used (
9.35
) and (
9.29
).

Finally, we recall the exponential decay (
9.21
) and therefore we obtain from (
9.36
) that the following holds

(9.37)

\displaystyle\sup_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left|\psi-\underline{\psi}\right|^{2}\leq Be^{-c\tau}\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[n\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}[n^{2}\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}[n^{3}\psi]n^{\mu}.

We conclude (
6.7
) and the proof of the Corollary.

10.
Proof of Theorem
2
and Corollary
2.1

In Theorem
2
we study the inhomogeneous wave equation

(10.1)

\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi=F,

where
F
is a sufficiently regular function.

Let
T\geq 3
and let
3\leq\tilde{T}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{1}+T\leq\tau_{2}
. Let

(10.2)

\eta(t^{\star})=\eta^{(T)}_{\tau_{1}}(t^{\star}),\qquad\chi_{+}(t^{\star})=\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}(t^{\star}),\qquad\chi_{-}(t^{\star})=\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}(t^{\star}+\tilde{T}),

see Section
3
. We appropriately cut-off the solutions of the inhomogeneous wave equation (

) and we study the following equation

(10.3)

\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\left(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\right)=\eta\chi_{+}^{2}F+\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\eta+2\nabla^{\alpha}\eta\nabla_{\alpha}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi).

We use Carter’s separation of variables, see Proposition
3.1
, for the cut-offed equation (
10.3
) and obtain the fixed frequency ode

(10.4)

u^{\prime\prime}+\left(\omega^{2}-V\right)u=H,

where
u
here is the frequency localization of the function
\eta\chi_{+}^{2}\psi
, as explained in Section
3
, and

(10.5)

H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left(\rho^{2}\tilde{F}\right)^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell},\qquad\tilde{F}=\eta\chi_{+}^{2}F+\chi_{+}^{2}\psi\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\eta+2\nabla^{\alpha}\eta\nabla_{\alpha}(\chi_{+}^{2}\psi).

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Since the function
u
is a smooth solution of the inhomogeneous ode (
10.4
), and the boundary conditions (
3.17
) are satisfied, see Proposition
3.1
, we can use the fixed frequency result of Theorem
7.1
and obtain

(10.6)

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}(r-r_{+})^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}(\bar{r}_{+}-r)^{-1}\left|v^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)v\right|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}}{r^{3}}|v|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+\Delta^{2}\omega^{2}1_{\{(\omega,m,\tilde{\lambda}):r_{trap}\neq 0\}}|u|^{2}\right)dr

\displaystyle\leq B\Bigg(\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\epsilon}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u|^{2}dr+\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}|u^{\prime}-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u|^{2}dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\left(1_{\{|m|>0\}}|u|^{2}+\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}(\omega,m,\ell)}{r}\right)^{2}(\omega+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell})|u|^{2}\right)dr\Bigg)

\displaystyle+B\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)+B\left(\omega^{2}+m^{2}\right)|u|^{2}(+\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|H\right|^{2}+r\left|\left(g_{1}\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}+ig_{2}\right)H\right|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

where for

see (

). We sum (

and follow verbatim the steps of the proof of Theorem

, see Section

, to obtain the folllowing

(10.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\left|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{B}{T}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}|\eta\chi_{+}^{2}F|^{2}+\Delta|\mathcal{G}\left(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}F\right)|^{2},

where note that in the above (

.

Now, we want to extend (

, for some sufficiently small
\delta(a,M,l,\mu_{KG},j)>0
. We recall from Definition

that
\mathcal{G}
identically vanishes in
\{r\leq r_{+}\}\cup\{\bar{r}_{+}\leq r\}
, so the terms of (

are easy to extend to

. To extend the low order terms to the desired domain

we use Corollary
4.1
.

We conclude that for

and

such that for

we obtain that

(10.8)

\displaystyle b\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+b\int\int_{D_{\delta}(\tau_{1}+T,\tau_{2})}dg\Bigg(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}|W\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{+}}\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{T}\int\int_{D_{\delta}(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T)}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{-}}\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+\int\int_{\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}|\eta\chi_{+}^{2}F|^{2}+\Delta|\mathcal{G}\left(\eta\chi_{+}^{2}F\right)|^{2}

which concludes the proof of Theorem
2
.
∎

Now, we prove the higher order Corollary

Proof of Corollary
2.1
.

This is follows easily as a Corollary of Theorem

and of the higher order Corollary

(a corollary of the Morawetz estimate of Theorem
