11.
The axisymmetric case: A physical space proof

We use the generic constants

(11.1)

b(a,M,l,\mu_{\textit{KG}})>0,\qquad B(a,M,l,\mu_{\textit{KG}})>0.

with the algebra of constants

(11.2)

b+b=b\qquad b\cdot b=b,\qquad B+B=B,\qquad B\cdot B=B.

Recall from Section
5.4
that

(11.3)

\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}\big|_{m=0}

is a physical space vector field, and it takes the form

(11.4)

\mathcal{G}:=\mathcal{G}_{m=0}=\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mug_{1}(r)\partial_{r^{\star}}+G_{2}(r)\partial_{t^{\star}}

where

(11.5)

G_{2}=\begin{cases}\sqrt{G_{2}^{2}},\qquad r\geq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}\\
-\sqrt{G_{2}^{2}},\qquad r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}},\end{cases}

with
G_{2}^{2}=\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}-\min_{[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}
.

We need the following Lemma

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
.

We have that

(11.6)

\frac{2V_{\textit{axi}}G_{2}^{\prime}}{g_{1}(r)}\geq b\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r\Delta},

where
V_{axi}=\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta},\penalty\ g_{1}(r)=\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\sqrt{\Delta}}
, and moreover
G_{2}
is twice differentiable at
r_{\Delta,frac}
.

Proof.

Direct from the definitions of Section
5.4
.
∎

We now state the following theorem, for axisymmetric solutions
\psi
of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
). Note that there is no need to assume the assumption (MS).

Theorem 11.1
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Then, there exists a constant

(11.7)

C(a,M,l,\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}})>0

such the following holds.

Let
\psi
satisfy the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
) in
D(0,\infty)
. Then, we have the following

(11.8)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}\psi,\psi)+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}\psi,\psi)\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}\psi,\psi),

for any
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}
, where for the operator
\mathcal{G}
in the case of axisymmetry see Section
5
. For the energy density
\mathcal{E}(\cdot,\cdot)
see (
1.40
).

Proof.

We sketch the next steps, since they are similar to the arguments of
[
31
]
.

Let
\psi
be an axisymmetric solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
), namely

(11.9)

\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\psi=0,\qquad\partial_{\varphi^{\star}}\psi=0.

Note that for axisymmetric solutions
\psi
of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
) the following holds

(11.10)

\displaystyle\Box\phi\equiv-\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\rho^{2}\Delta}\left(\partial^{2}_{t^{\star}}{\phi}-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\partial_{r^{\star}}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r^{\star}}\phi\right)\right)+\frac{a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}{\rho^{2}\Delta_{\theta}}\partial_{t^{\star}}^{2}{\phi}+\frac{1}{\rho^{2}\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}\left(\Delta_{\theta}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\phi\right).

Therefore, the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.5
) in the case of axisymmetry, reduces to the following equation

(11.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\psi=\rho^{2}\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\psi,

(11.12)

\mathcal{R}\Psi\equiv-\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\left(\partial_{t^{\star}}^{2}\Psi-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\partial_{r^{\star}}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r^{\star}}\Psi\right)\right)+\frac{a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}{\Delta_{\theta}}\partial_{t^{\star}}^{2}{\Psi}+\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}\left(\Delta_{\theta}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\Psi\right),

where
\rho^{2}=r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta
.

Now, we note the following commutation

(11.13)

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{R},g_{1}\partial_{r^{\star}}+G_{2}\partial_{t}\right]\psi

\displaystyle=\partial_{r^{\star}}^{2}\psi\left(2V_{\textit{axi}}g_{1}^{\prime}-g_{1}V_{\textit{axi}}^{\prime}\right)+2V_{\textit{axi}}G_{2}^{\prime}\frac{\partial_{t^{\star}}{\mathcal{G}\psi}}{g_{1}}+\partial_{t^{\star}}^{2}{\psi}\left(-2G_{2}^{\prime}\frac{G_{2}}{g_{1}}V_{\textit{axi}}+g_{1}V_{\textit{axi}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+\partial_{t^{\star}}{\psi}\left(V_{\textit{axi}}G_{2}^{\prime\prime}+V_{\textit{axi}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\prime}G_{2}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi\Big(V_{\textit{axi}}g_{1}^{\prime\prime}+V_{\textit{axi}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\prime}g_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle-g_{1}V_{\textit{axi}}^{\prime}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}-g_{1}V_{\textit{axi}}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}\Big),

where
V_{\textit{axi}}=\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}.

Τhe components of the second order terms
\partial_{t^{\star}}^{2}{\psi},\partial_{r^{\star}}^{2}\psi
in (
11.13
) are identically zero, see Section
5
. We obtain

(11.14)

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{R},g_{1}\partial_{r^{\star}}+G_{2}\partial_{t}\right]\psi

\displaystyle=2V_{\textit{axi}}G_{2}^{\prime}\frac{\partial_{t^{\star}}{\mathcal{G}\psi}}{g_{1}}+\partial_{t^{\star}}{\psi}E_{1}(r^{\star})+\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi E_{2}(r^{\star}),

where

(11.15)

\displaystyle E_{1}(r^{\star})

\displaystyle=\left(V_{\textit{axi}}G_{2}^{\prime\prime}+V_{\textit{axi}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\prime}G_{2}^{\prime}\right),

\displaystyle E_{2}(r^{\star})

\displaystyle=\Big(V_{\textit{axi}}g_{1}^{\prime\prime}+V_{\textit{axi}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\prime}g_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle-g_{1}V_{\textit{axi}}^{\prime}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}-g_{1}V_{\textit{axi}}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}\Big).

Therefore, in view of (
11.14
) we compute

(11.16)

\displaystyle\Box\mathcal{G}\psi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\left[\mathcal{R},\mathcal{G}\right]\psi-\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\mathcal{R}\psi+\mathcal{G}\Box\psi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\left[\mathcal{R},\mathcal{G}\right]\psi-\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\rho^{2}\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\psi+\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\mathcal{G}\psi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\Bigg(2V_{\textit{axi}}G_{2}^{\prime}\frac{\partial_{t^{\star}}{\mathcal{G}\psi}}{g_{1}}+\partial_{t^{\star}}{\psi}\left(V_{\textit{axi}}G_{2}^{\prime\prime}+V_{\textit{axi}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\prime}G_{2}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}\psi\Big(V_{\textit{axi}}g_{1}^{\prime\prime}+V_{\textit{axi}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\prime}g_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle-g_{1}V_{\textit{axi}}^{\prime}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}-g_{1}V_{\textit{axi}}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\rho^{2}\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\psi+\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\mathcal{G}\psi.

We use the divergence Theorem, see Lemma
2.3
, with multiplier
W=\partial_{t^{\star}}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi^{\star}}
, see Lemma
2.2
, to the equation (
11.16
), where recall
\rm{K}^{\partial_{t^{\star}}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi^{\star}}}=0
, see Lemma
2.2
, in view of axisymmetry

(11.17)

\partial_{\varphi^{\star}}\psi=0.

We conclude that

(11.18)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{W}_{\mu}[\mathcal{G}\psi]n^{\mu}+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}2\frac{V_{\textit{axi}}G_{2}^{\prime}}{\rho^{2}g_{1}}\left|\partial_{t^{\star}}{\mathcal{G}\psi}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{W}_{\mu}[\mathcal{G}\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\textit{l.o.t.},

for all
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
where

(11.19)

\displaystyle\textit{l.o.t.}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\left(E_{1}(r^{\star})\partial_{t}\psi+E_{2}\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi\right)\left(\partial_{t^{\star}}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi^{\star}}\right)\mathcal{G}\psi-\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\rho^{2}\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\psi+\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\mathcal{G}\psi

with
\rho^{2}=r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta
. Moreover, note that

(11.20)

\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\textit{l.o.t.}\leq\frac{B}{\epsilon}\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}|\partial_{t^{\star}}\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\psi|^{2}+B\epsilon\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta r}|W\mathcal{G}\psi|^{2},

where for the vector field
Z^{\star}
see Section
2.5
,

Now we use (
11.18
) and Lemma
11.1
in conjunction with a Poincare type estimate, which we do not present here (for a fixed frequency such result see Lemma
7.5
) to obtain the following inequality

(11.21)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{W}_{\mu}[\mathcal{G}\psi]n^{\mu}+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta r}\left|\partial_{t^{\star}}{\mathcal{G}\psi}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{W}_{\mu}[\mathcal{G}\psi]n^{\mu}

for all
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
.

We use elliptic estimates to generate the following derivatives

(11.22)

\Delta|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}\psi|^{2}

on the left hand side of equation (
11.21
), by multiplying the equation that
\mathcal{G}_{m=0}\psi
satisfies, see (
11.16
), with
\mathcal{G}_{m=0}\psi
, and then using integration by parts. Then, we can generate a large non-degenerate
\Delta(\partial_{t^{\star}}\psi)^{2}
integrand term on the left hand side of equation (
11.18
) by using the positivity of the term

(11.23)

\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta r}\left|\partial_{t^{\star}}{\mathcal{G}\psi}\right|^{2}.

We obtain the following

(11.24)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta r}|\partial_{t^{\star}}\mathcal{G}\psi|^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r(r^{2}+a^{2})}|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{G}\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{t^{\star}}\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{W}[\mathcal{G}\psi]n^{\mu}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

which immediately concludes

(11.25)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}\psi,\psi)+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}\psi,\psi)\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}\psi,\psi),

for all
\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}
.
∎

It is easy to use an iteration argument, see
[
31
]
, to obtain

(11.26)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int\int_{\{t^{\star}\geq\tau\}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}\psi,\psi)\leq C\Bigg(\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G}\psi,\psi)\Bigg)\cdot e^{-c\tau}.

for some constants
c,C>0
. We can also obtain pointwise bounds from the above.

Appendix A
A classical Coifman–Meyer commutation estimate

We define the symbol class and the operator class we use in this paper.

Definition A.1
.

We define the symbol class

(A.1)

S^{1}=\{\sigma:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\exists C>0\penalty\ \forall 0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 1\penalty\ \left|\partial^{i_{1}}_{\omega}\Delta^{i_{2}}_{m}\sigma(\omega,m)\right|\leq C\left(1+|\omega|\right)^{1-i_{1}-i_{2}}\left(1+|m|\right)^{1-i_{1}-i_{2}}\},

where
\Delta_{m}
is the finite difference operator.

Now, we define the following function space

(A.2)

X=\{\Psi:\mathbb{R}\times[0,2\pi)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}:\penalty\ \Psi\penalty\ \text{is smooth},\penalty\ \supp\Psi\subset(a,b)\times[0,2\pi)\penalty\ \text{for some}\penalty\ |a|+|b|<\infty\}.

Let
\sigma\in S^{1}
, see Definition
A.1
. We define the following Fourier multiplier operator

(A.3)

\left(\textit{Op}(\sigma)\Psi\right)(t,\varphi):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}g(\omega,m)e^{i\omega t}e^{-im\varphi}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(\Psi)d\omega,

for any
\Psi\in X
, where here we define

(A.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(\Psi)(r,\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}e^{-i\omega t}e^{im\varphi}\Psi(t,\varphi,r,\theta)d\varphi dt.

The function
Op(\sigma)\Psi:\mathbb{R}\times[0,2\pi)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is also smooth.

We define the operator class

(A.5)

OPS^{1}=\{Op(\sigma):X\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,2\pi)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}),\penalty\ \sigma\in S^{1}\}.

The following result is a classical pseudodifferential commutator estimate

Lemma A.1
.

Let
P\in\textit{OPS}^{1}
. Then, there exists a constant
C(P)>0
such that if
\chi:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a smooth function, then we have the following.

For any smooth
f\in X
we have the following pseudodifferential commutation

(A.6)

\mskip 2.0mu|[P,\chi]f\mskip 2.0mu|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,2\pi))}\leq C\mskip 2.0mu|\chi\mskip 2.0mu|_{\text{Lip}}\cdot\mskip 2.0mu|f\mskip 2.0mu|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,2\pi))},

where
\mskip 2.0mu|\chi\mskip 2.0mu|_{\text{Lip}}=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{h\not{=}0}\frac{|\chi(t+h)-\chi(t)|}{|h|}
.

Proof.

The proof follows by slighlty adapting the proofs found in the book of Taylor [
[
42
]
, Chapter 4] (also see
[
15
]
), in conjunction with pseudodifferential estimates on the torus
[0,2\pi)
, see
[
35
]
and specifically see [Theorem 5.2,
[
35
]
]. See also the Appendix of
[
47
]
, where in fact the authors discuss pseudodifferential estimates on general smooth manifolds.
∎

Remark A.1
.

We emphasize that the constant
C=C(P)
, on the RHS of the pseudodifferential estimate (
A.6
), does not depend on the functions
\chi,f
.

Appendix B
Pseudodifferential commutation estimates on the Kerr–de Sitter spacetime

In Lemma
B.1
we prove several commutation properties for the operators
\mathcal{G},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
, see Definitions
1
,
5.4
respectively.

We will prove the estimates of Lemma
B.1
by using the standard theory for pseudodifferential operators (which we discussed in Section
A
) and Lemma
5.1
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Let
\mathcal{G},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
be as in Definitions
1
,
5.4
respectively.

Then, there exist constants

B(a,M,l)>0,\qquad C(a,M,l)>0

where the constant
C=C(a,M,l)>0
blows up in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
but is finite for the value
a=0
, such that the following hold.

Let
\chi
be either equal to
\chi^{2}_{+}
or
\eta\chi_{+}^{2}
, see Section
2.11
. Let
f:\penalty\ \mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be smooth such that

(B.1)

\supp f\subset\{0\leq t^{\star}<+\infty\}

and either
f\in L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
or
f\in H^{1}(\mathcal{M})
, depending on the particular inequality below. Then, we have the following:

(B.2)

\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\chi]f\right|^{2}\leq C\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}|f|^{2},

(B.3)

\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}|(\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})f|^{2}\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}|f|^{2},

(B.4)

\displaystyle f=0,\quad[\partial_{\varphi},\mathcal{G}]f=0,\quad[\partial_{\theta},\mathcal{G}]f=0,

(B.5)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}|[\partial_{r^{\star}},\mathcal{G}]f|^{2}\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[f]n^{\mu},\qquad\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta|[Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}]f|^{2}\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[f]n^{\mu},

(B.6)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left|[\partial_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\chi]f\right|^{2}\leq C\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[f]n^{\mu},

(B.7)

\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\frac{1}{\Delta}\left|[W\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\chi]f\right|^{2}\leq C\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[f]n^{\mu}+|f|^{2},

(B.8)

\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|[Z^{\star}\mathcal{G},\chi]f\right|^{2}\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[f]n^{\mu}+|f|^{2},

where for the vector field
Z^{\star}
see Section
2.5
. All the spacetimes volume forms of the spacetime domains of the present Lemma are to be understood as the volume form (
2.17
) defined in Section
2.7
.

Proof.

In the present proof the constant
B
, will only depend on
a,M,l
and moreover will not blow up in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
.

Furthrmore, we will use
C
to denote constants that only depend on
a,M,l,\mu_{KG}
, specifically they do not depend on the coordinate
r
, and will blow up in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
, but are finite for
a=0
.

Let
\alpha>0
be sufficiently small. We first prove inequality (
B.2
), where note that we divide our analysis
\alpha
–close the horizons
\mathcal{H}^{+},\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
and away from the horizons.

We note that for
\chi(t^{\star})=\chi(t^{\star}(t,r))
as in the assumptions of the present Lemma we have that there exists a constant
B(a,M,l)>0
such that for any
r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
we have that

(B.9)

\mskip 2.0mu|\chi\mskip 2.0mu|_{\text{Lip}}(r)\leq B,

since there exists a constant
B
such that for any
r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
and for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
we have
|\partial_{t}\chi(t,r)|\leq B
. We used the Lipschitz norm
\mskip 2.0mu|\chi\mskip 2.0mu|_{\text{Lip}}(r)=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{h\not{=}0}\frac{|\chi(t+h,r)-\chi(t,r)|}{|h|}
. Note that
\partial_{\varphi}\chi\equiv 0
.

The inequality
(
B.2
)
away from the horizons

\mathcal{H}^{+},\penalty\ \bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}

First we prove inequality (
B.2
) away from the horizons. We recall from Lemma
5.1
, that there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 1
and for any
r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
we obtain that

(B.10)

\left|\partial^{i_{1}}_{\omega}\Delta^{i_{2}}_{m}\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,r)\right|\leq C\left(1+|\omega|\right)^{1-i_{1}-i_{2}}\left(1+|m|\right)^{1-i_{1}-i_{2}},

for
i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 1
and therefore

(B.11)

\widetilde{g_{2}}(\cdot,\cdot,r)\in S^{1}=\{\sigma:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\exists C>0\penalty\ \forall 0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 1\penalty\ \left|\partial^{i_{1}}_{\omega}\Delta^{i_{2}}_{m}\sigma(\omega,m)\right|\leq C\left(1+|\omega|\right)^{1-i_{1}-i_{2}}\left(1+|m|\right)^{1-i_{1}-i_{2}}\}.

Note that the constant
C>0
of (
B.11
) does not depend on
r
and blows up in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
but is finite for the value
a=0
. Therefore, in view of the assumptions on the functions
f,\chi
, we can use the Coifman–Meyer commutator of Lemma
A.1
and conclude that

(B.12)

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}+\alpha}^{\bar{r}_{+}-\alpha}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\chi]f\right|^{2}\sin\theta d\varphi dtd\theta dr

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{r_{+}+\alpha}^{\bar{r}_{+}-\alpha}\mskip 2.0mu|\chi\mskip 2.0mu|_{Lip}(r)\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|f|^{2}\sin\theta d\varphi dtd\theta dr

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}|f|^{2},

where in the last inequality we used the bound (
B.9
).

The inequality
(
B.2
)
near the horizons

\mathcal{H}^{+},\penalty\ \bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}

Now, we prove inequality (
B.2
) near the horizons. We will use the result of Lemma
5.1
. Note that there exist smooth functions
h_{1}(r),h_{2}(r)
such that the following holds

(B.13)

\partial_{r^{\star}}=h_{1}(r)\left(\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}\right)+h_{2}(r)X

with
h_{1}(r)=1+\mathcal{O}(\Delta)
and
h_{2}(r)=\mathcal{O}(\Delta)
, where
X
here is a smooth vector field on the domain
\{r_{+}\leq r\leq\bar{r}_{+}\}
where
[X,\partial_{t}]=[X,\partial_{\varphi}]=0
.

Therefore, by using (
B.13
) we note the following

(B.14)

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\alpha}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\chi]f|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\alpha}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|[g_{1}\partial_{r^{\star}}+i\textit{Op}(\widetilde{g_{2}}),\chi]f|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\alpha}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left|[g_{1}h_{1}(r)\left(\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}\right)+i\textit{Op}(\widetilde{g_{2}})+g_{1}h_{2}(r)X,\chi]f\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\alpha}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left|[g_{1}h_{1}(r)\left(\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}\right)+i\textit{Op}(\widetilde{g_{2}}),\chi]f\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\alpha}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left|[g_{1}h_{2}(r)X,\chi]f\right|^{2},

where in (
B.14
) we suppressed the volume form
v(r,\theta)d\varphi dtd\theta dr
for brevity, where for
v(r,\theta)
see Section
2.7
.

The second term on the last line of (
B.14
) is easily bounded by a physical space commutation

(B.15)

\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\alpha}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left|[g_{1}h_{2}(r)X,\chi]f\right|^{2}\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}|f|^{2}.

To bound the first term on the last line of (
B.14
) we use Lemma
5.1
where recall that we proved that for any
r\in[r_{+},r_{+}]
the symbol

(B.16)

-g_{1}(r)i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)+i\widetilde{g_{2}}(\omega,m,r),

belongs in the symbol class

(B.17)

S^{1}=\{\sigma:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\exists C>0\penalty\ \forall 0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 1\penalty\ \left|\partial^{i_{1}}_{\omega}\Delta^{i_{2}}_{m}\sigma(\omega,m)\right|\leq C\left(1+|\omega|\right)^{1-i_{1}-i_{2}}\left(1+|m|\right)^{1-i_{1}-i_{2}}\},

where the constant
C>0
of (
B.17
) blows up in the limit
a\rightarrow 0
but is finite for the value
a=0
. Therefore, in view of the assumptions for the functions
f,\chi
(see (
B.9
)) we can use the Coifman–Meyer commutator estimate of Lemma
A.1
to obtain

(B.18)

\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{+}+\alpha}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left|[g_{1}h_{1}(r)\left(\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}\right)+i\textit{Op}(\widetilde{g_{2}}),\chi]f\right|^{2}\leq C\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}|f|^{2}.

By following similar considerations we also conclude that

(B.19)

\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\alpha}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\chi]f|^{2}\leq C\int_{\bar{r}_{+}-\alpha}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|f|^{2}.

Therefore, from (
B.12
), (
B.18
), (
B.19
) we conclude that

(B.20)

\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\chi]f\right|^{2}drdtd\not{g}\leq C\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}|f|^{2}.

Therefore, we conclude inequality (
B.2
).

The inequality
(
B.3
)

The second inequality of (
B.2
) is immediate by noting the following

(B.21)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left|(\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})f\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left|(\textit{Op}(g_{2})-\textit{Op}(\widetilde{g_{2}}))f\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{r_{+}}^{\bar{r}_{+}}dr\int_{0}^{\pi}\sin\theta d\theta\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m}\left|\left(g_{2}-\widetilde{g_{2}}\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}.

where note that above we used the coarea formula, see Section
2.9
. It is a straightforward computation to conclude inequality (
B.3
) from (
B.21
) after using the property (
5.65
) from Lemma
5.1
for the difference
\widetilde{g_{2}}-g_{2}
.

The identities
(
B.4
)

The identities (
B.4
) follow from pointwise considerations, in view of the definition of
\mathcal{G}
, see Definition
1
.

The inequalities of
(
B.5
)

We now prove the estimates (
B.5
). We only proceed to prove the last, which is the most elaborate, and note that the remaining inequality can be proved similarly. We proceed as follows

(B.22)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta|[Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}]f|^{2}=\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta|Z^{\star}\mathcal{G}f-\mathcal{G}Z^{\star}f|^{2}

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m}\Delta^{2}\left|Z^{\star}(g_{1}\partial_{r^{\star}}+ig_{2})\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)-(g_{1}\partial_{r^{\star}}+ig_{2})Z^{\star}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m}\Delta^{2}\left|Z^{\star}(g_{1})\partial_{r^{\star}}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)+iZ^{\star}(g_{2})\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m}\Delta^{2}\left|\frac{dg_{1}}{dr}\partial_{r^{\star}}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)+i\frac{dg_{2}}{dr}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m}1_{\mathcal{SF}}\Delta^{2}\left|\frac{dg_{1}}{dr}\partial_{r^{\star}}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)+i\frac{dg_{2}}{dr}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m}1_{(\mathcal{SF})^{c}}\Delta^{2}\left|\frac{dg_{1}}{dr}\partial_{r^{\star}}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)+i\frac{dg_{2}}{dr}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

where in the first similarity we used the coarea formula, see Section
2.9
, and Parseval identities. For
\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}
see (
3.1
).

By recalling the definitions of
g_{1},g_{2}
, see Definition
5.2
, we bound the
non-superradiant frequency terms
of the last line of (
B.22
), namely the last term of the last line of (
B.22
). We note that the following holds

(B.23)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m}1_{(\mathcal{SF})^{c}}\Delta^{2}\left|\frac{dg_{1}}{dr}\partial_{r^{\star}}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)+i\frac{dg_{2}}{dr}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}1_{(\mathcal{SF})^{c}}\frac{\Delta^{2}}{\Delta^{3}}\left|\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}1_{(\mathcal{SF})^{c}}\Delta\left(|\omega\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)|^{2}+|am\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)|^{2}\right),

where in the last inequality we used that
\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\sim\mathcal{O}(\Delta)\left(\omega^{2}+(am)^{2}+Z^{\star}\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)
near
r_{+},\bar{r_{+}}
, since
\supp f\subset\{0\leq t^{\star}<+\infty\}
.

Finally, we bound the
superradiant frequency term
of the last line of (
B.22
), namely the second to last term on the lat line of (
B.22
). We recall from (
7.34
) that for the superradiant frequencies we obtain that
\frac{d}{dr}(g_{2})=-\text{sign}\left(r-r_{s}\right)\cdot\frac{d}{dr}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)g_{1}(r)\right)
. We bound

(B.24)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m}1_{\mathcal{SF}}\Delta^{2}\left|\frac{dg_{1}}{dr}\partial_{r^{\star}}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)-i\text{sign}\left(r-r_{s}\right)\cdot\frac{d}{dr}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)g_{1}(r)\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}1_{\mathcal{SF}}\Delta^{2}\left|\frac{dg_{1}}{dr}\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}-i\text{sign}\left(r-r_{s}\right)\cdot\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}+

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}1_{\mathcal{SF}}\Delta^{2}\left|\frac{d}{dr}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)g_{1}(r)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}1_{\mathcal{SF}}\sqrt{\Delta}\left|\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}-i\text{sign}\left(r-r_{s}\right)\cdot\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}1_{\mathcal{SF}}\Delta^{2}\left|\frac{d}{dr}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)g_{1}(r)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2},

where recall that

(B.25)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}1_{\mathcal{SF}}\left|\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}-i\text{sign}\left(r-r_{s}\right)\cdot\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}1_{\mathcal{SF}}\Delta\left(|\omega\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)|^{2}+|am\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)|^{2}\right),

where, again, in the last inequality we used that
\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\sim\mathcal{O}(\Delta)\left(\omega^{2}+(am)^{2}+Z^{\star}\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)
near
r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}
, since
\supp f\subset\{0\leq t^{\star}<+\infty\}
.

Therefore, by (
B.23
), (
B.24
) we conclude that for the superradiant frequencies the following holds pointwise

(B.26)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}1_{\mathcal{SF}}\Delta^{2}\left|\frac{dg_{1}}{dr}\partial_{r^{\star}}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)-i\text{sign}\left(r-r_{s}\right)\cdot\frac{d}{dr}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)g_{1}(r)\right)\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m}1_{\mathcal{SF}}\Delta\left(|\omega\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)|^{2}+|am\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\mathcal{F}_{\omega,m}(f)|^{2}\right).

Therefore, by using (
B.22
) and Parseval identities, in view of the pointwise bounds (
B.23
), (
B.26
) we conclude the last estimate of (
B.5
).

The inequalities
(
B.6
), (
B.7
), (
B.8
)

The inequality (
B.6
) is immediate since

(B.27)

[\mathcal{G},\partial_{\theta}]=0

and therefore

(B.28)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left|[\partial_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\chi]f\right|^{2}=\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left|[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\chi]\partial_{\theta}f\right|^{2}\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

where and in the last inequality we used the pseudodifferential commutation (
B.2
) already proved earlier.

For the inequality (
B.8
) we proceed as follows

(B.29)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|\left[Z^{\star}\mathcal{G},\chi\right]f\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|\left[[Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}]+\mathcal{G}Z^{\star},\chi\right]f\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|\left[[Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}],\chi\right]f\right|^{2}+\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|\left[\mathcal{G}Z^{\star},\chi\right]f\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|\left[[Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}],\chi\right]f\right|^{2}+B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|\mathcal{G}(fZ^{\star}\chi)+\chi[\mathcal{G},Z^{\star}]f\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Delta\left|\left[[Z^{\star},\mathcal{G}],\chi\right]f\right|^{2}+\left|\chi[\mathcal{G},Z^{\star}]f\right|^{2}+\Delta|\mathcal{G}(fZ^{\star}\chi)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int\int_{\mathcal{M}}J^{n}_{\mu}[f]n^{\mu}+|f|^{2},

where in the last inequality we use the already proved (
B.5
) to bound the first integral, and note that the last integral is bounded easily. The inequality (
B.7
) follows similarly.
∎
